# DECISIONS.md — the decision ledger

**Rules (ruling 26):** append-only. One line per ruling: number, date, tag, decision, amends-pointer if any, context link. Amendments are NEW entries pointing back — never edits. When any spec/brief and this ledger disagree, the ledger wins; code is ground truth over every document; a ledger-vs-code disagreement is a finding, never a silent divergence. Briefs cite rulings by number — they may quote verbatim with the number, never paraphrase.
**IDs — date-slug keys for every NEW row (2026-08-16).** Write the id as `YYYY-MM-DD-<kebab-slug>`,
e.g. `2026-08-16-ledger-ids`. **Why the change:** the old rule ("the appender claims the next free
number at write time") made collisions structural — two lanes opened the same day both take the
next integer, and whichever merges second must renumber AND chase every cross-reference that cited
the number. That happened **three times in one night** (rows 120/121/122) on lanes that touched no
common code; every conflict was this table and this table alone. A date-slug cannot collide unless
two lanes pick the same slug on the same day, and if they do, `check-decision-guards.cjs` now FAILS
on the duplicate instead of leaving a human to spot it. **The numeric series 1–122 is frozen, not
deprecated** — those ids are cited across the briefs, CLAUDE.md and the guard registry, and must
never be renumbered or reused. Cite old rulings by number and new ones by slug; both are permanent.
Rulings issued in conversation remain PROVISIONAL until appended here, and the ledger is still the
id authority.

**Tags (ruling 26 §3, sharpened by 27):** `[guarded: <check-name>]` = enforced by a machine check that **runs in CI on every push/merge** (a script not wired into CI is not a guard — it is MISSING). `[advisory]` = judgment/process ruling, ledger only. `deferred:<lane>` marks a guard owed by a named lane; the ledger lint warns until the lane merges, then fails.

| 38 | 2026-08-06 | [advisory] | **Checkout atomicity = CLAIM → AUTHORIZE → PROMOTE, reclaim-by-TTL.** `POST /api/checkout` committed every irreversible effect (booking rows, cart clear, `purchased` flip + diary row, provider notification + EMAIL) BEFORE `paymentIntents.create`, the one operation that can fail; on failure it answered a bare 500 and the same key short-circuited to `{duplicate:true}` which the cart page rendered as "Booking created!" — a checkout with no PaymentIntent reported as success, unrecoverable because with no PI there is never a webhook. **The claim ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING and stays** (§15; measured: 3 concurrent same-key checkouts = 3 real Stripe charges without the `service_bookings.idempotency_key` claim, 1 with it — commit `c3a0be03`), so option 1 ("write nothing until the PI exists") was REJECTED as a §15 deletion, and option 2 (compensating rollback) was REJECTED because rollback code runs in exactly the conditions that broke the operation. Ratified shape: the claim stays pre-Stripe but PROVISIONAL, and every commitment moves behind the authorization. **No new state:** `status='payment_pending' AND stripe_payment_intent_id IS NULL` is already an unauthorized claim by construction and consumers already key on that column — no migration, no enum value, no publish-push trap. TTL = **30 min**, reclaimed by an idempotent, race-safe, **logged** sweep (`checkout_claim_expired` diary row per void, rulings 12/16/18). Race safety = atomic conditional `WHERE status='payment_pending' AND stripe_payment_intent_id IS NULL` on BOTH the void and the authorization stamp, so a promote and a void can never both win. **A paid booking can never be voided**, via two layers: (1) a pre-flight `bookingDetails.stripeAttemptAt` marker written before the Stripe call — an unmarked row provably never reached Stripe and is voided with no network call; (2) marked rows are NEVER auto-voided, only reconciled against Stripe (`paymentIntents.list` on the `bookingIds` metadata) — found ⇒ PROMOTED, definitively-absent ⇒ voided, unreachable ⇒ QUARANTINED. Both legacy-table reconciliation paths (`handlePaymentSucceeded`, `POST /api/bookings/confirm-payment`) are inert for cart checkout and are filed as tasks #212/#213 — the sweep is designed assuming they stay broken. Option 3 (traveler-facing resume surface) stays a later enhancement. Journey J1/J2's payment leg flips from "declared unavailable ⇒ assert the committed rows anyway" (which encoded the defect) to the truthful pair `assertCheckoutAccepted` / `assertCheckoutCommittedNothing`; permanent Tier-3 negative **N16** + `checkout-claim-sweep.db.test.ts` (9 behavioural proofs). | checkout-atomicity lane, ratified in chat 2026-08-06 |
| 39 | 2026-08-06 | [advisory] | **Executes 38's filed tasks #212/#213 — money-path redundancy restored: ONE payment promotion, TWO callers.** Ruling 38 recorded that BOTH documented reconciliation paths were inert for cart checkout and designed the TTL sweep "assuming they stay broken"; that left the sweep as the **only** recovery mechanism for a crashed-mid-authorization checkout. Root cause confirmed exactly as filed: `handlePaymentSucceeded` (`stripe-payment.service.ts:564`) and `POST /api/bookings/confirm-payment` (`bookings.ts:125` → `booking.service.ts:592`) both query the LEGACY `bookings` table with `service_bookings` ids — disjoint id spaces, zero rows, no error. **New finding (refines, does not contradict, 38):** the webhook ROUTE was not wholly inert — `webhooks.routes.ts` carried an inline "Step 2" `UPDATE service_bookings … WHERE stripe_payment_intent_id = <pi> AND status='payment_pending'`, a SECOND divergent promotion with no diary row, no actor, no plan catch-up, and — keyed exclusively on a stamped PI id — no help in the one window only the webhook can rescue. 38's framing still holds: that path cannot see a claim whose PI was never stamped. **Shipped:** `promotePaidCheckout` (step 5 of `checkout-claim.service.ts`), driven by BOTH callers plus the route's Step 2 (deleted). Idempotency = the atomic conditional `WHERE status='payment_pending' AND stripe_payment_intent_id=<pi>` (the §15 discipline, never check-then-update), so a double signal is exactly ONE flip and ONE diary row. **Webhook-only capability:** only a signature-verified delivery may resolve bookings from `pi.metadata.bookingIds` and stamp a PI onto an unstamped claim (via the sweep's own `stampAuthorization`) — a client-supplied PI never can. **Late signal vs voided row:** never a resurrection (void wins after TTL) — it lands in a **reconciliation-exception** state, recorded as a DB fact (`bookingDetails.reconciliationException` + `checkout_reconcile_exception` diary row + `logger.error`) and surfaced at `GET /api/admin/bookings/reconciliation-exceptions`. Diary vocabulary gains `actorType='webhook'` and two event types (code-only — migration 171 deliberately carries no CHECK); **no migration, no schema change, no publish-push trap.** The legacy `bookings` rail stays live and untouched (still reached via `/booking-demo` and `/itinerary-comparison/:id` → `POST /api/bookings/process-cart`) — retiring it is a separate, unfiled question, NOT executed here. Permanent Tier-3 negatives **N17/N18/N19** (`checkout-payment-promotion.db.test.ts`, 10 proofs); N16 and the sweep's 9/9 suite unchanged and green; CLAUDE.md **§15c**. | legacy-reconciliation lane, 2026-08-06 |
| 40 | 2026-08-06 | [advisory] | **DETECTION made two-eyed: one drift job, both rails — and it DETECTS, it does not repair.** Rulings 38/39 made RECOVERY three-layered (TTL sweep · webhook · client confirm, one shared promotion), but DETECTION stayed one-eyed: `server/jobs/stripeReconciliation.ts` scanned ONLY the legacy `bookings` table, so **cart-checkout charges — the primary checkout — were invisible to the daily Stripe-vs-DB drift job**, matching zero rows without erroring (the same disjoint-id-space failure ruling 39 fixed on the promotion side). Nothing in the platform told anyone that money and the database disagreed about a cart purchase. **Shipped:** one job scanning BOTH rails with the id linkage that already exists — `service_bookings.stripe_payment_intent_id`, `pi.metadata.bookingIds`, the `idempotency_key` sibling convention, `refunds.stripe_refund_id`, and the legacy `charge.metadata.bookingId` — **no new writes anywhere on the checkout path**. Nine classifications (seven cart, the two legacy ones preserved verbatim in behaviour): `pi_succeeded_no_booking`, `pi_succeeded_claim_provisional`, `pi_succeeded_booking_voided`, `booking_confirmed_no_pi`, `booking_confirmed_pi_not_succeeded`, `amount_mismatch`, `refund_not_reversed`, `stripe_charge_no_booking`, `booking_no_stripe_charge`. **Ops surface = PERSISTED ROWS, not log lines** (migration 177, declared in `shared/schema.ts` same commit per the deploy-push rule): `reconciliation_exceptions` is APPEND-ONLY with a UNIQUE `dedupe_key` + `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so a month-long drift is ONE row stamped with the run that first saw it while the run row separately records detected-vs-new; `reconciliation_runs` carries **one row per pass INCLUDING a clean or skipped one**, because "no drift today" and "the scheduler died three weeks ago" were previously the same picture. Surfaced at `GET /api/admin/reconciliation/exceptions` + `/runs` on the existing `/admin/reconciliation` page — a **SIBLING** of ruling 39's `GET /api/admin/bookings/reconciliation-exceptions`, not a replacement: that one can only describe a row that exists, this one can describe money with no row behind it. **DETECT, DON'T REPAIR is the governing rule** — the job never voids, refunds, cancels or invents a booking; repair stays with the three recovery layers plus a human. **ONE narrow exception:** a PI-succeeded / still-provisional claim is handed to the EXISTING shared `promotePaidCheckout` with `actor="reconciliation"`, diary-logged — recovery layer 2's own logic arriving late, not new repair code. **AMENDS 39 on one clause:** its ordering-1 capability (resolve bookings from `pi.metadata.bookingIds` and stamp a PI onto an unstamped claim) was written "webhook only" because the signed delivery was then the only server-verified Stripe source; it is now gated on `SERVER_VERIFIED_ACTORS` = {webhook, reconciliation}, since the drift job reads the PaymentIntent from the Stripe API with the platform's OWN secret key — the same authority, arguably stronger (a pull, not a push). **The clause that does NOT move: a CLIENT-supplied PaymentIntent may never resolve or stamp anything** (still proven by N17c). The expected charge amount is SERVER-DERIVED as `SUM(total_amount + platform_fee)` over the PI's rows (§14) with a rounding tolerance that is the checkout's own arithmetic, not a rate — no fee literal. Legacy-scan disposition: the legacy rail is **still live** and its scan is **kept**, per §15c. Permanent Tier-3 negatives **N20/N21/N22** (`reconciliation-detection.db.test.ts`, 15 proofs); the sweep's 9/9 and the promotion suite's 11/11 are untouched and green; CLAUDE.md **§17**. | reconciliation-detection lane, 2026-08-06 |
| 41 | 2026-08-06 | [advisory] | **Ratifies 40, and states its invariant as PROVENANCE, not transport.** Ruling 39 wrote the ordering-1 capability (resolve bookings from `pi.metadata.bookingIds` and stamp a PI onto an unstamped claim) as "webhook only"; ruling 40 opened it to `SERVER_VERIFIED_ACTORS` = {webhook, reconciliation}. The generalization this ratification records, so the clause is never re-litigated actor by actor: **what gates the capability is the PROVENANCE of the PaymentIntent id — that the platform itself obtained it from Stripe as a verified actor — NOT the transport that carried it.** A signature-verified webhook delivery (a push) and the drift job's authenticated read with the platform's own secret key (a pull) are the same authority; the transport differs and is irrelevant. Two consequences follow: a future server-verified actor joins `SERVER_VERIFIED_ACTORS` without amending the principle, and no actor qualifies merely by arriving over a trusted-looking channel. **The clause that does NOT move, and is the whole reason for framing it this way: a CLIENT-supplied PaymentIntent id may never resolve or stamp anything** — a client id has no server provenance regardless of how it arrives, including over an authenticated session. Already proven by the existing Tier-3 negative **`N17c: a CLIENT may not stamp a PaymentIntent onto an unstamped claim`** (`server/__tests__/checkout-payment-promotion.db.test.ts:280`, asserting `promoted.length === 0`), verified present at `d4f59bb7`; **no new negative was built** — the client half of the invariant already carries its permanent proof. CLAUDE.md **§17b**. | provider-sigma lane Phase 0, rider (a) |
| 42 | 2026-08-06 | [guarded: check-money-endpoints, phase2-fee-gate] | **Provider money hardening — a RATE is client-trusted input, a PROVISIONAL claim is not the owner's to accept, and a consumerless irreversible endpoint is DELETED.** Executes the three findings the provider-sigma Phase-0 audit rated CRITICAL/HIGH (`docs/testing/PROVIDER_SIGMA_AUDIT.md`, audited@`d4f59bb7`; all three re-verified as STILL HOLDING at `9382d500`). **(1) MI-1 — rates resolve from `fee_bands` ONLY, and a rate-bearing field is never client-settable on ANY schema.** `provider_services.revenueShareRate` was exposed by `insertProviderServiceSchema`, parsed straight off `req.body` by POST **and** PATCH `/api/provider/services`, spread into the row, and read at `payments.routes.ts` as *"the final override (takes priority over config)"* over the `fee_bands`-resolved split at the real Stripe charge — a client-supplied rate reaching a payment decision (§14 in substance, §8 in spirit; the clamp was range-only, so `1.00` ⇒ a 0.00 platform fee). The audit found it stripped on NEITHER create nor update. **Required shape = STRIP-AND-CLAMP, two layers:** the schema omits it (layer 1) and `createProviderService`/`updateProviderService` strip it and DERIVE the value in STORAGE so every caller is covered (layer 2) — the same placement rationale as the approval-lifecycle strip. Derivation is ONE call into the EXISTING `resolveCommissionRates` with the same option shape /api/checkout uses (new thin helper `resolveServiceOwnerShareRate`); **no second rate implementation.** **Class sweep, not the instance:** every `createInsertSchema` in `shared/schema.ts` was crossed against every schema `.parse`d from a request body — exactly FOUR are client-parsed. Beyond `revenueShareRate` this stripped the whole rate/fee + payment-identity family on `insertLocalExpertFormSchema` (`bookingFeeType|Percentage|Fixed|Hourly`, `minBookingFee`, `feeSettings`, `stripeAccountId`, `stripeAccountStatus`, `stripeConnectStatus`, `canReceivePayments`, `totalEarnings`, `pendingPayout`, `payoutSchedule` — all mass-assignable at POST /api/expert-application, all dormant, which is why they had never surfaced); `serviceCategories.commissionBandKey` is admin-by-design and annotated, not stripped. **(2) SD-1 — a provisional claim is UNACCEPTABLE INPUT: rejected, never promoted.** `PATCH /api/provider/bookings/:id/status` checked the TARGET status and never the CURRENT one, so a provider clicking Accept on a `payment_pending`/unstamped claim (§15b — visible to them in Inbox and titled "Booked" on their calendar) promoted a purchase nobody had paid for, after which `voidClaim` AND `promotePaidCheckout` both matched ZERO rows and the claimed `vendor_availability_slots.booked_count` was destroyed with no code path back. The owner rail now carries a FROM-state allow-list, and the write is the §15 **atomic conditional** (`UPDATE … WHERE id = ? AND status IN (<expected>)`, new optional `expectedFromStatuses` on `updateServiceBookingStatus`; omitting it preserves every existing caller verbatim) — the pre-check is only the error message, the transition itself is the guard. **Sole author of claim-machine mutations remains `checkout-claim.service.ts`**; this is the owner rail REFUSING to touch a row that machine owns, not a second implementation. SD-2 (provider cancel of a paid booking issues no refund) is deliberately UNCHANGED — still unruled. **(3) AC-1 — DELETED, not hardened.** `POST /api/vendor-availability/:id/book` was `storage.bookSlot(req.params.id)` behind `isAuthenticated` and nothing else: any account could exhaust any provider's inventory, no booking row meant the TTL sweep had nothing to reclaim, and `releaseSlot` had no reachable caller. Zero consumers, proven at `9382d500`. An endpoint with **no consumer and an irreversible effect is deleted** — gating it would have preserved a second, unaudited way to consume inventory beside the checkout spine. `storage.bookSlot` survives as checkout's atomic claim. Proven by `server/__tests__/provider-money-hardening.db.test.ts` (**P1–P6**, 6 proofs: insert-strip and update-strip with the persisted rate equal to a `fee_bands` row READ FROM THE DB and an admin band edit moving it per ruling 32; provisional-accept rejected with row/slot/diary unchanged; the once-strandable row still reclaimable by the sweep AND promotable by `promotePaidCheckout`; 5 concurrent accepts ⇒ exactly ONE winner; route-inventory absence). Sweep 9/9, promotion 11/11 (incl. **N17c**), detection 15/15 untouched and green; tsc holds at 197. No migration, no schema DDL, no publish-push trap. | provider money-hardening lane, 2026-08-06 |

| 46 | 2026-08-06 | [guarded: check-money-endpoints] | **A booking may never be BORN carrying a PaymentIntent — and PRIVILEGED-FIELD MASS-ASSIGNMENT is now a STANDING CLASS whose required fix shape is STRUCTURAL.** Executes `#PS15`, filed by the provider money-hardening lane's own class sweep (ruling 42 / `PROVIDER_SIGMA_AUDIT.md` §8.2) and re-verified as STILL HOLDING at `281d355c`. **(1) The finding is a VIOLATION, not a tension.** Ruling 41's immovable clause — *"a CLIENT-supplied PaymentIntent id may never resolve or stamp anything"* — stands with **no carve-out**: `stripePaymentIntentId` is a `SERVER_VERIFIED_ACTORS`-only field, written **solely** via the shared promotion path (`stampAuthorization` / `resolveAndStamp`, `checkout-claim.service.ts`). `POST /api/bookings` (`routes.ts`) `.parse`d the `.omit()`-based `insertServiceBookingSchema` off `req.body` and **SPREAD** it into `createServiceBooking`, so a crafted request could BIRTH a booking already carrying its own PaymentIntent. Ruling 41 was proven on the PROMOTION side by **N17c**; a born-stamped row is **not a promotion**, runs no promotion code, and therefore could never trip it — yet it looks authorized to every consumer keyed on that column (the TTL sweep skips it as not-an-unstamped-claim, `promotePaidCheckout` matches it, the drift job trusts it as linkage). N17c watches the door; PS15 came through the wall. **Fix = the MI-1 shape, three layers:** `insertServiceBookingSchema.omit({stripePaymentIntentId})` (layer 1 — safe outright, since NO caller of `createServiceBooking` passes the field and the column's sole production writer is `stampAuthorization`; the audit's caution that the omit list is load-bearing for the `InsertServiceBooking` TYPE is true of `platformFee`/`insuranceFee`/`providerEarnings`/`status`, which checkout DOES pass, and **not** of this field), a storage-level strip in `createServiceBooking` (layer 2 — covers the two internal `as any` callers a type-level omit cannot reach, and logs when it fires), and the route-level **pick-based allowlist** below. **(2) THE STANDING CLASS, third instance.** `revenueShareRate`, the MI-1 sweep's dormant fee/payout family on `insertLocalExpertFormSchema`, and this. Recorded as a class: **a privileged column becomes client-settable by DEFAULT under a denylist schema, and nobody edits an omit list for a column that did not exist when it was written.** **The required fix shape is STRUCTURAL: allowlist (pick-based) schemas, so new privileged columns are unreachable by default.** Applied here — `createBookingRequestSchema` (`shared/schema.ts`) admits exactly `serviceId, tripId, contractId, bookingDetails, bookingMetadata`; `status`, `idempotencyKey`, `slotId`, the amount family and the PI are all unreachable, and `totalAmount` + the split are now SERVER-DERIVED (§14 from the catalog row; §8 from `fee_bands` via the EXISTING `resolveServiceOwnerShareRate` — ruling 42 rule 1, derivation delegates). The route previously trusted `req.body.totalAmount` for the row while using `service.price` for the revenue counter on the next line, so the two could disagree by any amount the caller chose. **(3) SCHEMA-POSTURE VERDICT + follow-up.** All **186** `createInsertSchema(...)` calls in `shared/schema.ts` are `.omit()`-based; **ZERO** are `.pick()`-based. Converting the layer is filed as **`#PS18`**, NOT executed here, and ships with its committed negative fixture (**B6**: the same fake privileged column, reachable under `.omit()`, unreachable under `.pick()`) built while the trace was fresh. **(4) The rows already on disk get DETECTION, never silent trust or silent repair** — a fix stops new rows and says nothing about old ones, and old ones are the detector's job (§17). New drift classification **`payment_provenance_unverified`** (`warning`, cart rail), surfaced at `GET /api/admin/reconciliation/exceptions` on `/admin/reconciliation`. **Its predicate follows ruling 41's invariant as STATED — PROVENANCE, not one implementation of it — so TWO independent forms of server provenance each clear a row:** the §15b pre-flight `bookingDetails.stripeAttemptAt` marker (the spine wrote it), **or** Stripe's own `metadata.bookingIds` naming this booking when the job reads the PaymentIntent with the platform's own secret key (a `SERVER_VERIFIED_ACTORS` read, §17b). Corroboration is not a forger's loophole: `metadata.bookingIds` is written server-side by `createPaymentIntent`, so a real PaymentIntent lifted from elsewhere names the bookings it actually paid for and never the row it was planted on, and a PaymentIntent absent from Stripe is never seen at all (**B4b** proves both halves). A marker-only predicate would have indicted every legitimate booking whose PI predates ruling 38 but which Stripe can still vouch for — **and, found by building it, would have flagged the detection suite's own fixtures**, which is how the second form was discovered. Blast radius is bounded by `loadCartBookings`' existing window scoping. **(5) Guard extended, with fixtures (ruling 43).** `check-money-endpoints.cjs`'s schema-mediated pass now carries a PAYMENT-IDENTITY column predicate (`stripe<Thing>Id` / `paymentIntentId`) beside the rate one — deliberately narrow (a `status` or a grant is NOT covered; ruling 42 stripped that family by name), with 10 new committed `--self-test` fixtures and its negative space restated: it knows the RATE and PAYMENT-IDENTITY classes and nothing else, which is exactly why the structural answer is `#PS18` and not a wider grep. Proven catching the pre-fix shape in isolation. **ARCHAEOLOGY: NOT ANSWERABLE FROM THIS LANE** — the shared dev bench is still owner-side and unreachable (`#PS17`, audit §8.6), so the counts are ZERO against a schema-only local Postgres and prove the QUERY, not the population. The two paths that actually flip a listing to publicly-live (`approval_status='approved' AND status='active'`, per `storage.ts:1649/1994` and `storefront.routes.ts:557`) were both completely ungated: (1) **admin approval** (`storage.approveProviderServiceListing`, the canonical way an expert listing goes live) set `approvalStatus:"approved"` AND `status:"active"` in one write with no verification check at all — an admin approving an unverified expert's listing published it, the exact outcome ruling 53 exists to prevent; (2) the owner's own **Activate toggle** (`PATCH /api/expert/services/:id/status`) accepted `status:"active"`, ownership-checked but not verification-checked. **Decision-maker's choice, ratified: option (B)** — admin approval is ALLOWED against an unverified owner (preserves the admin's review work; refusing it would waste a real review) but must NOT make the listing publicly live; go-live then follows the owner's own verification automatically. **Mechanism, no schema/enum/migration change (publish-trap avoidance, CLAUDE.md):** admin approval always sets `approvalStatus:"approved"`; it sets `status:"active"` ONLY when the LISTING OWNER (never the acting admin) already satisfies the same predicate the original gate checks; otherwise the row lands `approvalStatus:"approved" AND status:"draft"` — **held, not live, by construction**. This is distinguishable from owner intent for the same reason the §15b "unauthorized claim by construction" pattern works: the owner's own Pause toggle writes `status:"paused"`, never `"draft"` (verified: `client/src/components/backoffice/my-offerings-table.tsx:302`), so `approved+draft` unambiguously means "the gate held it" and `approved+paused` unambiguously means "the owner chose to pause it" — the sweep below reads exactly that distinction and must never conflate the two. **ONE predicate, extracted:** `resolvePublishVerification(userId)` (`server/services/publish-verification.service.ts`) carries the exact role/verification branch logic ruling 53 specified (admin bypass; provider = identity AND business via `service_provider_forms`; expert = identity only via `local_expert_forms`, `businessVerified: null`; anything else = default-deny) as the ONE definition of "may publish" — `checkPublishVerificationGate` (`server/routes.ts`) now calls it and only shapes the 403 body, so the two original call sites (`:2366` POST, `:2539` PATCH) are UNCHANGED in behavior (all 11 f2-verification-gate proofs stay green verbatim). **New enforcement points, all resolving through the same function, never re-implemented:** admin approval (`server/routes/admin.routes.ts`'s `POST /api/admin/provider-services/:id/approve`, evaluated against `service.expertId` — the owner, not `adminId`); the owner's Activate toggle (`PATCH /api/expert/services/:id/status`, gated whenever the target is `"active"`, same as the original two sites). **Auto-activation sweep:** `activateVerificationHeldListings(userId)` (same service file) re-evaluates the predicate and promotes that user's `approved+draft` rows to `active` — and is proven to leave an `approved+paused` row for the same user untouched, and to be idempotent (a second pass activates zero rows). Wired from the two verification-write paths that can newly satisfy the predicate: `storage.updateFormIdentityVerification` (called from the Stripe Identity webhook, `server/routes/webhooks.routes.ts:66`) and the Stripe Connect `account.updated` business-verification derivation (ruling 55) in the same file, both fire-and-forget/non-fatal so a sweep failure never fails the webhook 200. §13: the sweep fails CLOSED — a predicate that cannot be evaluated (lookup miss, wrong role) activates nothing. Proven by `server/__tests__/publish-verification-hold.http.test.ts` (9 proofs: unverified-expert admin-approve held+not-public, verified-expert admin-approve live+public, both repeated for a provider incl. a partial-verification provider still held, the owner-toggle block/allow pair, the sweep's held-activates/paused-never-touched/idempotent triple, and a fails-closed no-op) — all 11 original f2-verification-gate proofs verified still green alongside it. | 41fee08; QA_PUNCH_LIST.md P0; ruling 53; ruling 55 |

Two more committed-but-unwired suites sat beside them: `service-deliverable.http.test.ts` (9 proofs, D3 entitlement + the pre-purchase leak-prevention sweep) and `short-links-frame.http.test.ts` + `posting-opportunities-frame.http.test.ts` (7 + 2 proofs, D4 frame dedupe/analytics and opportunity→frame mapping). All five are `.http.test.ts` suites (`node:test` + raw `fetch()`), not Playwright — they need Postgres AND a real HTTP server on :5000, no browser. **New workflow, not an extension of an existing one:** `.github/workflows/publish-gate-and-fundamentals-gate.yml`, job/status-check name **`publish-gate-and-fundamentals (DECISIONS.md rulings 53, 56; D3/D4)`**, triggered `pull_request`/`push` to `main` (blocking, same shape as `fee-resolution-authority-gate.yml`). This workflow is journey-suite's server-boot recipe grafted onto fee-resolution-authority-gate's blocking trigger — the cheapest correct combination. **DB setup delegates to `./.github/actions/ci-db-setup`** (migrations + sessions table; `seed-ci-users: false` since every suite mints its own users via `POST /api/auth/register`, same posture as journey-suite) — never inlined, per the `ci-db-setup-lint` gate. **Env:** `DATABASE_URL` from the throwaway Postgres service; `JOURNEY_DB_WRITES_OK=1` (every suite's own disposable-DB guard refuses fixture writes without it); `NODE_ENV=production`, `SESSION_SECRET`, `ALLOW_TEST_ACCOUNTS=1`, `SESSION_COOKIE_INSECURE=1`, an `sk_test_` `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` stub (never `sk_live_` — CLAUDE.md's publish-trap note on `server/validate-env.ts`'s hard-stop), matching the boot env `app-routes-gate.yml`/`journey-suite.yml` already use. **`RATE_LIMIT_LOOPBACK_SKIP=1` is load-bearing, found by building this gate:** the five suites run as five SEQUENTIAL steps in one job rather than one combined `npx tsx --test a b c d e` invocation, because `node:test` runs multiple file arguments concurrently across worker threads by default and every suite registers fresh users from the same loopback IP in its `before()` hook — run together, reproduced locally at 28/38 failures, all `429 Too Many Requests` on registration; sequential steps plus the loopback rate-limit exemption (the same opt-out `journey-suite.yml` already carries, for the identical reason) eliminate the contention entirely, back to 38/38. Each suite step prints its own TAP pass/fail counts to the job log and a final always-run verdict step renders a per-suite pass/fail table into the run summary and fails the job if any suite failed — diagnosable from the log alone, no local re-run needed. **Negative space (ruling 43):** this gate proves exactly the 38 proofs in these five files and nothing wider. It does **not** exercise Stripe's real identity/business verification webhooks (`STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` is a non-functional `sk_test_` stub — Stripe is never actually called); does **not** cover the provider `ServiceForm` UI submit path (HTTP-only, no browser, no DOM); and does **not** prove the D3 deliverable file itself is access-controlled in storage — `service-deliverable.http.test.ts` proves the `serviceFile` FIELD is never leaked in any pre-purchase API response, but the file remains a pasted URL, unchanged by this gate (P1 punchlist entry, filed separately). **Rulings 53 and 56 are UNCHANGED — never edited (append-only, ruling 26).** They remain `[advisory]` rows exactly as written; THIS entry is the `[guarded: publish-gate-and-fundamentals]` one that carries their enforcement, read together with them going forward. | 58 | 2026-08-11 | [advisory] | **R4 lands the protected-deliverable rail: `objstore:<key>` STREAMS, a legacy pasted URL still REVEALS — closing the D3 gap ruling 41's PROMOTION-side provenance framing left open one product-layer up (QA_PUNCH_LIST.md P1).** D3 gated WHO could reach `GET /api/service-bookings/:id/deliverable` correctly (9 proofs, unchanged) but then `res.json({ fileUrl, ... })`'d the provider's pasted external URL verbatim — the entitlement gated a ONE-TIME URL REVEAL, not file access: once any buyer saw it, the link was permanently shareable, unrevokable, outside platform control. **Key-not-URL discipline (the trap this ruling avoids):** `server/infrastructure/object-storage.ts`'s pre-existing `uploadBuffer()` returns `objectPublicUrl(path)` — a raw `https://storage.googleapis.com/<bucket>/<key>` string. Stated honestly, not assumed either way: residual exposure if the bucket turns out to be public-readable is bounded by key unguessability alone, so storage keys embed a `crypto.randomBytes(16)` hex token (`deliverables/<serviceId>/<32-hex-chars>.pdf`) regardless of the finding — defense-in-depth that costs nothing and is never relied upon as the only control (the proxy path never calls `objectPublicUrl()` or discloses the key in any response). **Download log (R5):** new table `deliverable_downloads` (migration 194, declared in `shared/schema.ts` — publish-trap rule: booking_id/service_id/user_id FKs `ON DELETE CASCADE`, `protected` boolean, `downloaded_at`) gets exactly one row per SUCCESSFUL fetch (either shape), giving a future D8 "auto-complete after N days undownloaded" pass the download SIGNAL it does not yet have (P2) — this migration implements NO completion/auto-complete logic itself; D8 remains unruled. **Upload intake, no new dependency:** no multipart plumbing exists anywhere in this codebase (no multer/busboy/formidable — verified by grep; `vendor-management.service.ts`'s `uploadDocument` takes a buffer with no HTTP caller) — `POST /api/provider/services/:id/deliverable-file` uses `express.raw({ type: ["application/pdf","application/octet-stream"], limit:"20mb" })` scoped to that one route only, zero new npm dependencies. Owner-gated the same way `serviceImage` already is (session/ownership check, not a privileged §14/§18/§19 field — `serviceFile` needed no allowlist before and needs none now); validates the PDF by its `%PDF-` magic bytes (Content-Type is client-declared and not trusted alone) and a 20MB cap; degrades honestly with a `503 OBJECT_STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE` when the bucket env is absent — never a crash, never a fabricated success. **Test driver, no live Replit credentials required:** `object-storage.ts` gained an `OBJECT_STORAGE_DRIVER=memory` in-process `Map<string,Buffer>` driver, production-fenced by construction (`NODE_ENV !== "production"` required in addition to the env var) — and the real `@replit/object-storage` import was moved from a top-level `import` to a lazy `import()` inside `getClient()` specifically so the memory driver works even when that package cannot be installed at all (this sandbox's npm proxy blocks it; found while building this suite). `server/__tests__/deliverable-protected-rail.http.test.ts` (13 proofs) runs the REAL route code — upload then download — against the fake driver over real HTTP, asserting byte-for-byte equality between what was uploaded and what streams back, that the managed response carries no JSON/URL/key of any kind, that the legacy shape is unchanged plus `protected:false`, that entitlement negatives still 404 on a managed deliverable, and that `deliverable_downloads` gets exactly one row per success and zero on a 404. **Guard's negative space (ruling 43):** existing pasted-URL deliverables are NOT migrated — `provider_services` rows with a non-empty `serviceFile` lacking the `objstore:` prefix remain unprotected link-delivery by design (an owner must re-upload through the new endpoint to convert one); this is the same "detect, don't repair" posture as §17. | 66 | 2026-08-11 | [advisory] | **RULING 63 EXECUTED — D8 per-method completion is ONE machinery, six conditions; and the completion event turned out not to exist for ANY method, not just pdf.** **(0) THE FINDING THAT REFRAMES P2.** The punchlist described the gap as pdf-shaped ("a pdf booking reaches `completed` only via the manual path: provider flips status → held earning → confirm-completion releases it"). That premise is wrong in its first clause: **the provider CANNOT flip a booking to `completed`** — `OWNER_SETTABLE_BOOKING_STATUSES` is `["confirmed","cancelled"]` and the handler answers *"Completion is confirmed by the traveler"* (deliberate, to stop self-crediting), while `POST /api/bookings/:id/confirm-completion` **requires the booking to already BE `completed`**. Grep of every writer of `service_bookings.status='completed'` on `main` returns exactly ONE: `admin.routes.ts`'s dispute-REJECT. So the escrow completion path was a **closed loop — unreachable for every delivery method** — and no service booking outside a rejected dispute has ever minted a held earning. P2's "held earning on an inattentive traveler" was therefore the *smaller* half of the defect. **(1) ONE MACHINERY, N CALLERS** (`server/services/booking-completion.service.ts`), the §15c "one promotion, two callers" shape one lane over: `completeBooking()` is the ONLY place a D8 rule moves a booking to `completed`. It adds **no money logic whatsoever** — it re-resolves eligibility server-side, then calls the EXISTING `storage.updateServiceBookingStatus(id,'completed',reason,['confirmed'])`, which already owns the platform-revenue row and both held earnings. Fee bands, `checkout-claim.service.ts`, `promotePaidCheckout` and every rate/amount are **untouched**: this lane wires WHEN completion happens, never HOW MUCH moves (§8/§14/§18). **RELEASE is deliberately NOT part of completion** — the held earning matures through the existing escrow window or the traveler's existing early confirm, which is exactly what gives ruling 63's async row its "disputable window" **for free, with no second constant**: `holdWindowDays('service_booking')` is already both the earning's `availableAt` and the `POST /api/bookings/:id/dispute` cutoff. **(2) THE TABLE AS IMPLEMENTED.** `in_person`/`hybrid` → `confirm_completion`, **untouched by every D8 caller** (ruling 63: "as built"; the timer and the owner rail both refuse it with a stated reason). `property`/`property_room` → `checkout_date`, from the `bookingDetails.checkOut` snapshot checkout already writes, eligible once that DAY has fully passed (never mid-stay). `pdf` → `artifact_timer`, both arms as worded: **7 days after the FIRST `deliverable_downloads` row** (migration 194 — needs no new state, so it covers every row however old), **or 7 days undownloaded post-delivery**. `call`/`video`/`voice_notes` → `session_end`, provider-confirmed and **evidence-gated against the booked slot's own end time**. `async_messaging` → `provider_declared`. `bundle` → `bundle_components`: per-component declarations accumulate on the EXISTING `bookingDetails` jsonb (keyed against the purchase-time `bundleComponents` snapshot — a component not in that snapshot is REJECTED, never merged), and only the last one completes the booking. **Method resolution has ONE home**: `completionRuleFor` in `shared/service-fundamentals.ts`, beside the D2 predicates — the job and the route carry no method list of their own. **(3) THE OWNER RAIL'S SELF-CREDIT OBJECTION IS ANSWERED, NOT WAIVED.** `POST /api/provider|expert/bookings/:id/complete` is not a status write: the rule is server-resolved from the service row, an owner may declare only the three rules ruling 63 assigns them, `session_end` cannot be declared before the slot's end time has passed, and the flip mints a **held** earning whose clearance window IS the traveler's dispute window — so a wrong declaration is disputable and reversible before any money moves. Body is an explicit **allowlist of one field** (`componentServiceId`, bundles only); acting user from the session, booking by path + ownership, undifferentiated 404 for a non-owner (§14/§19). The `PATCH …/status` rail still refuses `completed` and was **not** loosened. **(4) TIMERS = §15 SHAPE.** `server/jobs/bookingAutoCompletion.ts` DETECTS and drives the shared function with an actor tag; hourly, beside the earnings-release scheduler. The flip's atomic conditional is the guard, so a double run, two overlapping passes, or a pass racing the owner produce exactly ONE flip, ONE earning set and ONE diary row — the loser is a no-op and **there is no compensating rollback anywhere in this lane**. `payment_pending` is never an accepted from-state: a provisional claim stays the claim machine's (§15b/§18b). **(5) OBSERVABILITY — the choice, stated (build charter §2): NO runs table.** Each flip records itself twice — `bookingDetails.completion` on the booking row (rule, actor, timestamp and the EVIDENCE the decision rested on; present even for a tripless booking) and an `item_transition_log` `booking_completed` row (new event type, new `provider`/`auto_complete` actor types — code-only vocabulary, no CHECK) when the booking carries a trip, which the diary requires by construction. Each RUN logs one structured line **including a run that did nothing**, so a dead scheduler is an absence of lines rather than a quiet day (§17 rule 2's spirit without its table). **(6) MIGRATION 196 — the only schema change**, one additive nullable `provider_services.deliverable_uploaded_at`, no CHECK, no backfill, declared in `shared/schema.ts` (publish-trap rule). It exists because ruling 63's undownloaded arm measures from POST-DELIVERY and nothing recorded when a deliverable landed (`updated_at` moves on any edit), so without it a provider uploading 30 days late would have had the timer fire at day 7 on a booking where **nothing was delivered**. It is a MONEY-TIMER field, so it is stripped in **both** §18 layers — `insertProviderServiceSchema.omit()` and a strip-and-DERIVE in `updateProviderService` that stamps it only when the deliverable value genuinely CHANGES (an unrelated re-save must not push every open booking's timer out). §13: NULL is never read as a value — that arm is skipped with the reason `no_delivery_timestamp`, while the downloaded arm still covers the row. **(7) NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43) — what this does NOT build.** **NO-SHOW POLICY IS NOT INVENTED HERE** — ruling 63 files it as its own follow-up and this lane touched none of it: a call whose slot ended is completable whether or not anyone attended, and there is no attendance signal, no no-show state and no no-show refund path. **No release logic** — completion never releases early, and `confirm-completion`/`releaseMaturedEarnings`/`reverseEarningsForBooking` are byte-for-byte unchanged. **No refund lane** — ruling 63 routes partial bundles to the EXISTING refund lane and this lane deliberately does not duplicate or extend it; a partial bundle simply never completes and pays out nothing. **No in-person fix** — that rule is "unchanged" by the ruling's own words, so the deadlock in finding (0) **remains open for `in_person`/`hybrid`** and is filed, not silently repaired under cover of this ruling. **No UI** — the owner-complete endpoint has no client surface yet. **No `voice_notes` path in practice**: D2 does not classify it as scheduled, so such a booking normally carries no `slot_id` and the session-end gate refuses it with `no_booked_slot` — reported as a gap, not papered over with a guessed session end. **No CI wiring** — hence `[advisory]`, the same gap rulings 58/64 recorded. Proven by `server/__tests__/booking-completion-machinery.db.test.ts` (**17/17**, ten of them negatives: both arms firing and both arms NOT firing early; the §13 no-delivery-timestamp and no-checkout-date skips; the double-run one-flip/one-earning/one-diary proof; a call booking untouched by the timer; a `payment_pending` claim never completed; in-person refused by every D8 caller; session-not-ended and no-slot both refused with zero earnings; async completed with earnings HELD and the EXISTING release job refusing to release inside the window; a partial bundle recorded-but-not-completed with zero earnings and a bogus component rejected; non-owner 404 / unauthenticated rejected; and the delivery clock proven un-settable by a client and derived server-side). Keep-green: f2-verification-gate 11, publish-verification-hold 9, service-deliverable 9, deliverable-protected-rail 13, service-logistics-capture 9, short-links-frame 7, posting-opportunities-frame 2, checkout-claim-sweep 9, checkout-payment-promotion 11, reconciliation-detection 15, booking-birth-provenance 7 — **102/102**. `check-money-endpoints.cjs`, `phase2-fee-gate.sh`, `check-unmounted-routers.cjs`, `check-decision-guards.cjs`, `check-omit-schema-ratchet.cjs` all exit 0; lockfile `replit.local` = 0; tsc holds at the CI-locked baseline **183**. | 67 | 2026-08-11 | [advisory] | **RULING 62's D9 CLAUSE EXECUTED — attestations keyed to method + category risk, with the D2 honesty shape; a TITLE-CLAIM affirmation, NOT a licensing gate.** Ruling 62, verbatim: *"D9: onboarding attestations keyed to method + category risk, inapplicable ones OMITTED with a reason — the D2 honesty shape at the top of the funnel."* Concrete first application is QA_PUNCH_LIST **SS-5** / SIX_SIGMA_PROVIDER_PASS §3 **R-1**: Japan **deregulated paid guiding on 2018-01-04** — no licence is required to trade and this platform must never behave as though one were — but the title 通訳案内士 (*Tsūyaku Annai-shi*) is legally protected and an unlicensed guide may not use a title implying official certification. Listing name and description are free text with nothing asking, so the honest intervention is an affirmation about the **CLAIM**, never a gate on the **TRADE**. **(1) THE APPLICABLE SET IS SERVER-DERIVED — a client never decides what applies to itself.** The keying predicate lives in ONE shared module, `shared/service-attestations.ts`, beside (and importing) the D2 predicates: `isPlaceAnchored`/`isClassifiable` are REUSED, never re-implemented, and this module adds only the CATEGORY axis. The server re-resolves the set from the LIVE row — delivery method, product shape, and the row's category **key AND slug** — on every read and again on every write; applicability is never stored, so a listing that changes delivery method never carries a stale "applies to you" claim. Both category identifiers are matched because the live taxonomy has rows with a `category_key` and rows with only a `slug` (e.g. Language & Translation), and keying on one alone would have silently missed half of it. This is §14's derive-it-server-side posture generalized from the AMOUNT to the APPLICABILITY. **(2) THE CATALOG — three types, each with a stated rationale, deliberately not a compliance matrix.** `title_claim_honesty` (SS-5): **guide/interpreting categories, EVERY delivery method** — a pdf itinerary sold by a "government certified guide" makes exactly the same claim, so the method axis must NOT gate it; `language-translation` is included on purpose because 通訳案内士 is literally an *interpreter*-guide title. `in_person_safety_basics`: **place-anchored methods, any category** — the only shape that puts a traveler physically in a provider's care, where the platform verifies nothing and carries no cover, and had never said so. `food_safety_disclosure`: **food categories AND place-anchored** — both axes, because a recipe pdf serves nobody. Each type carries EN+JA label and body; the JA is **machine-authored platform copy** and native review is filed on the EXISTING **I18N-1** debt, as a set with the ruling-65 glossary — this is legally-loaded copy in the launch market and must not be read as reviewed. **(3) §13 HONESTY — THREE states, not two.** APPLICABLE (rendered, affirmable); **OMITTED WITH A STATED REASON** when the row lacks the signal to decide (no category / no delivery method) — never guessed, never rendered as compliant, and **not affirmable**: affirming an undecidable key is a 400 carrying the reason, because recording a statement about a condition the platform cannot say applies is the dishonest artifact D9 exists to prevent; and SILENCE when we know it does NOT apply — no entry at all, the `delivery_asset` precedent in `provider-listing-health.routes.ts` (an omission entry still reads as "this could apply to you"). A decision we CAN make on one axis outranks missing data on the other, so only genuine ignorance produces an omission. Each reason names **what ITS OWN missing signal would have decided** and is LOCALIZED like the catalog copy — both of those were defects found by driving the real wizard (one generic sentence rendered twice, and rendered in English inside an otherwise-Japanese card), not by reading the code. **(4) STORAGE — migration 197, `service_attestations`**, child rows of `provider_services` on the `dmo_extracted_places`/`service_route_points` pattern: ON DELETE CASCADE, **UNIQUE (service_id, attestation_key)** — and that UNIQUE is the idempotency MECHANISM, not a nicety (the write is `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so a replay keeps the FIRST affirmation's timestamp and never re-dates the record). `affirmed_by` is the SESSION user, ON DELETE SET NULL so deleting an account never erases the historical fact. No DB CHECK on the key (app-enforced vocabulary — the migration-144/195 posture, publish-trap avoidance); table + UNIQUE + index declared in `shared/schema.ts` (publish-trap rule). Chosen over a jsonb column because an attestation is an evidentiary record that must be joined to a user and read back years later (ruling 13's class-B posture), and because rows make idempotency structural rather than application logic. **(5) WRITE PATH — owner-gated, §19 ALLOWLIST, append-only.** `GET/POST /api/provider/services/:id/attestations` (`server/routes/service-attestations.routes.ts`, MOUNTED). The body is a hand-written zod object of exactly ONE field (`affirm: string[]`) — deliberately NOT a `createInsertSchema(...).omit()` over a table whose every other column is server-stamped, which is precisely the denylist shape ruling 46 named; **no `createInsertSchema` call was added, so the #PS18 ratchet count is untouched at 190.** `affirmedAt`/`affirmedBy` are stamped in storage from the session; an unknown key, an inapplicable key or an undecidable key each 400 with a distinct code and **one bad key refuses the WHOLE batch** (no partial write). Non-owner gets an undifferentiated 404. **(6) CAPTURE SURFACE — the ServiceForm wizard's "Terms & requirements" step** (the C9 precedent: per-listing curation belongs to the "what I sell" module), in a NEW fully-i18n-wrapped component `client/src/components/provider/service-attestations-card.tsx`. It renders only when the shared resolver returns a non-empty applicable set; an already-affirmed row is checked, DISABLED and date-stamped (a statement made, not a setting). The affirmation is a child row, so it is written by a second call AFTER the listing save; a failure there does NOT report the save as failed (§13) but raises its own honest toast. **Omission surfacing — the judgment, stated:** omissions render as ONE muted de-duplicated line INSIDE the card and only when something is already affirmable, because every omission here is fixable in step 1 of this same wizard; a listing with nothing applicable shows no card at all rather than a page of caveats. **The wizard's own toast stays hardcoded English on purpose** — ServiceForm is on I18N-2's "migrate the WHOLE surface in one commit" list and a single `t()` there would half-wrap it; the new card is a new file and is wholly wrapped. **(7) NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43) — what this deliberately does NOT do, each filed rather than silently skipped.** **NO PUBLISH GATE** — nothing here can block `status:'active'`; whether an unattested listing should be blocked is NOT ratified and is filed as a decision-maker question (**SS-5a**). **NO TRAVELER-FACING SURFACE** — no trust badge, no attestation on `/services/:id`; displaying these to travelers is its own ruling (**SS-5b**). **No licence-document upload or verification**, **no admin review queue change**, **no scanning of listing text for violating phrases** (**SS-5c**) — that last one is the arm that would actually catch a lie, and it is a different, unruled product. **No un-affirm/DELETE path** — the record is append-only. **No CI wiring** — hence `[advisory]`, the same gap rulings 58/64/66 record for their committed suites. Proven by `server/__tests__/service-attestations.http.test.ts` (**13/13**, eleven of them refusals: the pdf-vs-in-person and food-vs-remote keying negatives; the omitted-with-reason §13 proofs incl. per-key distinctness and JA presence; inapplicable, undecidable and unknown keys each refused with nothing written; one-bad-key-refuses-the-batch; client-supplied `affirmedAt`/`affirmedBy`/`userId`/ `serviceId` all ignored; non-owner 404 on both verbs and unauthenticated rejected; three CONCURRENT replays yielding one row with the first timestamp plus a direct proof that the DB UNIQUE really rejects a second row; and an affirmation whose key stopped applying still reported). **Reachability was proven in the real browser, not just the API** (the SS-1 standing lesson — *proved correct, never proved reachable*): the card renders in the live wizard for four keying cases incl. Japanese, and a real checkbox + the real Save button persisted a row end-to-end. Addresses QA_PUNCH_LIST **SS-5** (build commit `f0ac5a0`); the not-built arms are filed as **SS-5a** (publish gate), **SS-5b** (traveler display) and **SS-5c** (listing-text scanning). | ruling 62 (D9); QA_PUNCH_LIST.md SS-5 + SS-5a/b/c; docs/findings/SIX_SIGMA_PROVIDER_PASS.md §3 R-1; migration 197; `shared/service-attestations.ts`; `server/routes/service-attestations.routes.ts`; `client/src/components/provider/service-attestations-card.tsx`; `server/__tests__/service-attestations.http.test.ts` |
| 68 | 2026-08-11 | [guarded: fee-resolution-authority] for the resolution + attribution clauses asserted in the CI-wired suites; [advisory] for the rest | **RULING 61 EXECUTED — D6 rails attribution: the chain now EXISTS, and it starts default-deny.** **(0) THE AUDIT THAT SHAPED IT.** The resolver half was already built and CI-pinned (rulings 47/48/51: rails is min(category band, rails), rails waives the traveler fee, a bandless category throws) — but `resolveProviderRate`'s `isRails` flag **had no caller anywhere in the repo**, and `fee_ledger` (migration 179) **had no writer at all**. So "rails" existed as a resolver capability with nothing able to switch it on and nowhere to record it. This lane builds ONLY the missing links: capture → validation → resolver input → ledger stamp. **(1) THE REF IS A SELECTOR, NEVER A VALUE (§14/§18).** The client already relayed a captured `?ref=` into the checkout body (S4/migration 141, analytics-only per ruling 45) and **no client code changed** — `server/services/rails-attribution.service.ts` re-reads that opaque token against `short_links` and re-authorizes it against THIS traveler and THIS listing's owner. It can express neither a rate nor an amount; a forged token buys nothing. The RATE is **ONE call into the existing single resolver** (§18 rule 1 — min(band, rails) and the waiver stay inside it; this lane re-implements neither). **(2) WHAT THE PIN REFUSES, each degrading to the FULL rate and never to an error:** fabricated (`unknown_ref`), malformed, **expired** (`expires_at`, migration 198), **wrong-provider** (`owner_mismatch`), **self-referral** (a provider may not discount their own take by clicking their own link), non-provider owner, categoryless service, and a fail-loud band throw (`band_unresolvable` — an optional discount lane must never turn a breached R2 guard into a failed purchase). A per-entity override (ruling 47) outranks rails and deliberately does **not** carry the waiver along with it. Every refusal is recorded on the booking row (`bookingDetails.railsAttribution`, the ruling-66 tripless-booking precedent) **and** logged — never a silent grant, never a blocked purchase. **(3) OWNER MATCH, NOT TARGET MATCH — stated because it is a judgment.** Eligibility asks whether the link's OWNER owns the service being booked, so a provider's storefront/story link carries rails onto any of THEIR OWN listings in the same cart. It never crosses to another owner's listing. **(4) ONE DECISION, TWO LOOPS.** `/api/checkout` resolves per-item rates **twice** (quote loop and booking loop). The attribution decision is made ONCE in a pre-pass both loops read, so the amount quoted and the amount claimed cannot disagree. A rails line takes its share from the resolver and deliberately **outranks the per-service `revenueShareRate` snapshot** that ruling 47 dethroned; a non-rails line is byte-identical to before. **(5) THE CONSEQUENCE, STATED HONESTLY.** A rails booking is the FIRST charge path in the platform to price through the D1 single resolver; direct provider bookings still resolve the legacy `expert_standard` band pending the charge-path repoint (lane item 1C, ruling 45's "spec-ahead-of-code in its entirety"). So a rails booking today is cheaper for TWO reasons at once — the rails min() and the un-migrated direct path. Both numbers are decision-maker-ratified band values, the direction is always a SMALLER platform take (never an overcharge to a traveler or an underpayment to a provider), and it is reachable only through a provider's own validated link. **1C remains owed.** **(6) THE LEDGER — a rails-only slice, and it says so.** `server/services/fee-ledger.service.ts` is migration 179's first writer: ONE `provider_commission_rails` row per rails booking, `rate_source` stamped from the resolver (`rails`, or `band` in the premium case where the category band undercut rails — the fee TYPE names the LANE, `rate_source` names the BAND that decided), written at the **authorization stamp** (179's own strategy (b), so the PaymentIntent is in scope and a sweepable provisional claim never gets a row in an append-only table). Idempotent by a per-booking key on the existing UNIQUE index, driven by TWO callers (the inline post-authorization write and `promotePaidCheckout`'s catch-up for the server-died-mid-authorization window) — a double signal lands ONE row and the replay never rewrites the first recorded fact. **DEVIATION, DELIBERATE:** the waiver is **not** a zero row, because migration 179 CHECKs `amount <> 0` on purpose ("a zero-amount fee event … hides a resolution bug behind a real-looking row") — it rides the rails row as a first-class metadata fact-set (waived, basis, the band that priced it, the amount given up) carrying the §13 flag that the D3 traveler fee **is not billed on the direct path today**, so nobody may read the record as "we stopped charging them $X". **(7) NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43) — what this deliberately does NOT do.** **THE WAIVER MARKETING CAPTION STAYS OUT OF THE CAPTION ENGINE** — zero caption/share-image diffs in this lane; ruling 61 unlocks it only on a green pin, and scheduling it still needs a decision-maker go. **No analytics surface** (measurement stays on Performance, ruling 22d), **no rate-editing UI**, **no band value changed**, **no expert rails model invented**, **no `/r/:code` behaviour change** (an expired link still redirects and still counts its click; S4 analytics attribution is untouched — a click that happened is a true analytics fact whatever the fee lane says). `short_links.expires_at` has **no writer yet** (owner/admin expiry control filed) — it is declared read-side first because the alternative was to let ruling 61's "expired" refusal silently not exist. `fee_ledger` remains a RAILS-ONLY slice: direct checkout, the legacy rail, ready-mades, templates, coordination, tips and affiliate margin still write nothing, so the per-booking invariant is not yet assertable platform-wide — **do not aggregate the table as if it were complete.** **(8) PROOFS.** `server/__tests__/rails-attribution.db.test.ts` **R1–R11** (11) added to the CI-wired gate as a second step in the same job, plus **A9** in the gated resolver suite (9/9) — an admin edit to `provider_rails` reaches the next rails resolution, so the boolean selects a lane and never carries a rate. Migration 198 is one additive nullable timestamp, no CHECK, no backfill, declared in `shared/schema.ts` (publish-trap rule) and omitted from `insertShortLinkSchema` (§18 rule 3 — a money-timer field with no consumer is still stripped). Sweep 9/9, promotion 11/11, detection 15/15, provenance 7/7 and the rest of the 132-proof battery untouched and green. | dispatch v1.2 §1; ruling 61; CLAUDE.md §8/§13/§14/§15/§17/§18/§19 |

The full two-language text-scanning product, the false-positive policy and the enforcement/review queue stay FILED. **(6) 1C CHARGE-PATH REPOINT.** Direct provider bookings price through the **D1 resolver** (`resolveProviderRate` → category band) instead of the legacy `expert_standard`/`beta_flat` lane — the D1 resolver becomes the single authority on **every** charge path, closing the two-reasons-cheaper gap ruling 68 §5 made visible. Rails behaviour is unchanged: min(band, rails) and the waiver stay INSIDE the resolver (§18 rule 1 — delegate, never re-implement). **(7) SHARE-LINK EXPIRY — settable by the link's OWNER and by admin, nobody else.** Allowlist PATCH body (§19), `NULL` = never expires, a set value must be in the future. This gives ruling 68's `short_links.expires_at` (migration 198) its first writer; a default TTL on minting is deliberately NOT adopted. **(8) `voice_notes` → PROVIDER_DECLARED — AMENDS ruling 66's table.** It has no booked slot in the D2 vocabulary, so the `session_end` evidence gate could only ever refuse it (`no_booked_slot`); the async row is where it honestly belongs. **(9) SS TIER-B PRIORITIES.** **SS-4** (split pickup radius from service radius — an ADDITIVE column, never-clobber: `service_radius` untouched, no backfill, NULL renders as "not set") and **SS-6** (delivery-language capture on `provider_services`, distinct from ruling 60's chrome/content translation) are ratified to BUILD NOW; the rest of SS Tier B stays filed. **NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43) — explicitly NOT ratified and NOT in the execution lane:** deposits / partial payments on the cart rail (the schema fields exist but only the legacy rail has deposit code — a future ruling); traveler-facing attestation badges (disposition 4); the fee-waiver caption wording (disposition 2's hold); full listing-text scanning with an enforcement path (disposition 5's other half); and **billing the D3 traveler fee on the direct path** — disposition 6 moves the RATE only, so the traveler fee remains resolver-only and uncharged on the direct path exactly as ruling 68 §5 stated. | QA_PUNCH_LIST SS-4/SS-5a/SS-5c/SS-6 + the 1C, caption-unlock, expiry and voice_notes [DM] filings; rulings 61/62/63/66/67/68; decision-maker ballot Aug 11 |

On the rails lane a throw declines an optional discount; on the DIRECT lane the throw would be **the charge failing**, so a breached R1/R2 guard would take checkout down. A refusal therefore leaves the INCUMBENT legacy rate standing — logged at error level and recorded on the row as `bookingDetails.directRateResolution`, including the refusal reason, so a line that fell back SAYS so and nobody can read a green ledger as a D1 charge that never happened (§13). **PROOFS, in the CI-wired gate: A10** an admin band edit moves the next DIRECT resolution with no service-row and no category-row touch (A1's proof, one lane over); **A11** rails and direct on the SAME service now differ ONLY by ruling 48's min() and the traveler-fee waiver — both directions, including the premium case where the rates are identical and the waiver is the whole difference; **A12** the per-service `revenueShareRate` snapshot never outranks a resolved direct band (ruling 47's dethroning, finally holding off the rails lane too); **A13** expert lane / no category / no owner / breached band guard all refuse and leave the legacy share. `fee-resolution-authority.db.test.ts` **13/13** (was 9). **(2) IN-PERSON/HYBRID COMPLETION (disposition 1) — the closed loop is open.** New rule `service_date_timer` REPLACES `confirm_completion` in the D8 vocabulary; the traveler's `confirm-completion` is the EARLY RELEASE and is untouched (ruling 66 already separated the two). **N is not a new constant:** `serviceDateCompletionDays()` delegates to `holdWindowDays('service_booking')`, so completion lands exactly where the dispute window closes and cannot drift from it. **THE DATE SOURCE WAS AUDITED, NOT ASSUMED:** `service_bookings` has no `booking_date` column at all, so two server-side records answer, strongest first — the BOOKED SLOT (`slot_id` → `vendor_availability_slots.date`, the same evidence `session_end` gates on) then `bookingDetails.scheduledDate` (checkout's purchase-time snapshot). Nothing else: `createdAt` plus a guess is a guessed date minting real money. Eligibility is the DAY boundary + the window for both sources, so a same-day booking is never completed while the traveler could still be at the service. **NO DATE ⇒ not timer-eligible**, skipped with `no_service_date`, and in that case ONLY the owner rail's provider-declared arm opens — the SERVICE decides (`ownerDeclarableFallback`), the caller merely names its rail, and the row records `fallback: owner_declared_no_service_date`. A DATED in-person booking is refused on the owner rail exactly as pdf/property are (D8-N15). **THE DISPUTE CHECK NEEDED NOTHING ADDED and that was verified rather than assumed:** `POST /api/bookings/:id/dispute` writes `status='disputed'`, and `confirmed` is the only from-state any D8 rule may claim, so the existing atomic conditional already covers the new rule (D8-N12). **(3) `voice_notes` → PROVIDER_DECLARED (disposition 8)**, amending ruling 66's table: it has no booked slot in the D2 vocabulary, so the session-end evidence gate could only ever refuse it with `no_booked_slot` — the gap ruling 66 filed rather than papered over. Applicability stays server-derived (ruling 67 §1) from **the shape the listing will HAVE after the save** — the live row overlaid with the write's own fields — so the gate cannot be walked past by omitting a field. **§13 holds at the gate:** an UNDECIDABLE key (omitted-with-a-reason) never gates, because the platform may not demand a statement about a condition it cannot say applies (D9-G8). Affirmations may travel WITH the write (§19 allowlist `affirmAttestations`, re-validated exactly as the affirm endpoint validates it) for a structural reason: a child row cannot pre-date its parent, so a gate judged before a CREATE would otherwise make an applicable listing unpublishable forever. Deliberately narrow — ruling 67 noted "certified" is legitimate in compounds, and a matcher that fires on "PADI certified divemaster" teaches providers to ignore the warning, which is worse than not warning (proven by D9-W3). Its ABSENCE proves nothing. **(7) THE CAPTION (disposition 2) — reword now, claim later.** The NEUTRAL "Book direct through my link." line ships in `promo-text.service.ts` and its byte-identical client fallback; the FEE-WAIVER wording stays HELD, **and the AI prompt now explicitly forbids the model inventing it** — the caption engine was the one place a model could make the promise the ruling is holding back. The six-sigma two-labels-one-column notice is **RETIRED, not reworded** — it described a defect that no longer exists. **`delivery_languages`** closes SS-6, typed to match the AUDITED `local_expert_forms.languages` (jsonb string array); NULL = never captured and renders NOTHING on the traveler page — there is no presumed "English" — while `[]` = deliberately cleared is a different fact and stays distinguishable. The `GET /api/trips/:tripId/commission` earnings breakdown (`server/routes/booking-actions.ts`) is repointed the same way; the owner is fixed per request so the direct resolution is **memoised per category — no N+1**. | 72 | 2026-08-11 | [guarded: check-money-endpoints (§14/§18/§19a — the balance endpoint + the deposit PI-linkage columns), omit-schema-ratchet, phase2-fee-gate — all exit 0] for the money/mass-assignment invariants; [advisory] for the deposit checkout + balance flow (proven by the bench DB suite below, not CI-wired — the full cart checkout is the HTTP monolith) | **DEPOSITS / PARTIAL PAYMENTS ON THE CART RAIL — v1 LANDED (Lane 7).** Ratified design, built exactly: **MANUAL BALANCE + PROVIDER OPT-IN PER LISTING.** No auto-charge, no stored-card/off-session mandate, no scheduled job — each payment is its OWN authorized PaymentIntent inside the EXISTING §15 claim machine's one-intent-per-claim shape. **CONFIG LIVES ON `provider_services`, NOT `provider_pricing`** (audited: `provider_pricing` has ZERO server consumers — it is an orphan, so its `requiresDeposit/depositType/...` columns are NOT wired; reusing them would have been dead config). Migration **200** (additive-nullable, NO CHECK, all DECLARED in `shared/schema.ts` — publish-trap rule) adds `depositEnabled/depositType/depositPercentage/depositFlatAmount` to `provider_services` (owner LISTING config, like `price` — NOT a §8/§18 fee rate: nothing multiplies a platform take or selects a band; named so the fee gate cannot misread them) and mirrors the legacy `bookings` deposit/balance shape onto `service_bookings`: `deposit_amount/deposit_paid/balance_amount/balance_paid/balance_due_at/stripe_deposit_intent_id/stripe_balance_intent_id`. **BOOKING STATE MODEL — distinguishable BY CONSTRUCTION:** a deposit-partial booking lands in **`status='deposit_paid'`** (a plain varchar value OUTSIDE every paid-equivalent / completion-eligible set: `confirmed`/`in_progress`/`completed`/`disputed` and `COMPLETION_ALLOWED_FROM_STATUSES=['confirmed']` all exclude it), with `deposit_paid=true`, an outstanding `balance_amount>0`, and the deposit PI stamped on both `stripe_payment_intent_id` and `stripe_deposit_intent_id` — so it is distinct from a full-paid `confirmed` (which needs the balance) AND from an unauthorized `payment_pending` claim (which has NO PI). `total_amount/platform_fee/provider_earnings` stay the FULL values — deposit/balance are the PAYMENT SCHEDULE, not a re-split — so completion (D8) and earnings math are unchanged. **DEPOSIT CHECKOUT — the SAME claim machine, one deposit-aware seam:** the deposit line is an ordinary `payment_pending` claim (§15 sweep/authorize/promote unchanged); `promoteOneBooking` (the SHARED promotion the webhook/client/reconciliation/one-click all drive) now derives its TARGET from the ROW — a row with an outstanding balance promotes `payment_pending → deposit_paid` (not `confirmed`), a row without one is byte-identical to before (§13). The Stripe charge is the server-derived Σ(per-line deposit) + Σ(full non-deposit line); `resolveDepositPlan` (deposit.service.ts) is the ONE place a deposit amount is computed, from the listing config × the server-side line total (§14/§18 — never req.body). **BALANCE CHECKOUT — `POST /api/bookings/:id/pay-balance`** (owner-gated to the TRAVELER): its OWN PaymentIntent on `stripe_balance_intent_id`, a deterministic Stripe idempotency key (`bal-<id>`) so a retry cannot double-charge, an atomic `stampBalanceAuthorization`, and `promoteBalancePayment` — the SAME atomic-conditional shape as `promoteOneBooking`, PARAMETERISED onto the balance column + the `deposit_paid → confirmed` transition, NOT a second implementation of "confirmed". Amount is SERVER-DERIVED (`balance_amount` from the row); the webhook routes `metadata.isBalance` to this promotion (a balance PI on `stripe_balance_intent_id` would otherwise mis-resolve through `promotePaidCheckout`). **ESCROW/COMPLETION:** unchanged — the provider's earning matures on SERVICE COMPLETION via the EXISTING D8 machinery, NEVER on balance payment; a deposit-only booking is `deposit_paid` and releases NOTHING (proven). `balance_due_at` is derived from EXISTING facts (service date − change window / lead time), null when undatable (§13). **PROOFS — `server/__tests__/deposit-checkout.db.test.ts` D1–D9 (10 tests, all green):** D1 the deposit is server-derived, flip the config → the deposit moves; D2 deposit → `deposit_paid` (not confirmed), distinguishable from both; D3 balance → `confirmed`, `balance_paid=true`; D4a/D4b BOTH promotions idempotent (double signal = one flip, one diary row); D5 a `deposit_paid` booking is completion-ineligible (`wrong_status`) and mints NO earning; D6 (§15b) an unauthorized deposit claim is voided by the TTL sweep, never promoted; D7 (§13) a deposits-OFF line promotes straight to `confirmed` in one full flip; D8 (§14) `promoteBalancePayment` only flips on the row's OWN stamped balance PI (a forged PI → `balance_payment_intent_mismatch`); D9 the cutoff derivation. **§15 SPINE UNTOUCHED (no fork):** sweep 9/9, promotion 11/11, detection 15/15, provenance 7/7 green; fee-resolution 13/13 and rails-attribution 11/11 green (checkout money math intact). Guards exit 0; `tsc` 183 (unchanged); `replit.local` 0. ServiceForm gains a deposit config section (owner opt-in). **NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43):** NO auto-charge / off-session / stored-card mandate / scheduled job — balance is always a manual second checkout; NO new refund policy — overdue handling defers to the listing's EXISTING `cancellationPolicyType`; the OVERDUE-BALANCE SWEEP is DEFERRED to v2 (v1 builds DETECTION only — `GET /api/admin/bookings/balance-overdue` under the §2 blanket guard, and the state reads as `deposit_paid`, never `confirmed`, so the gap is visible not silent — §13); the legacy `bookings` rail is UNTOUCHED (its own deposit columns unchanged); `fee_bands` / the rate resolver / the claim machine's promotion spine are untouched (only the target-status derivation was made deposit-aware); DEPOSIT-AWARE RECONCILIATION is a filed follow-up (the drift job's §17 expected-charge = total+fee would flag a fully-paid deposit booking whose deposit PI covered only part — `deposit_paid` rows are skipped by the scan today, but a `confirmed` two-PI booking is not; deferred, does not affect the detection suite's fixtures). | Lane 7; rulings 38/39/40/42/46/63/66/69; CLAUDE.md §8/§13/§14/§15/§15b/§15c/§18/§19 |

(7) **Promote → Distribute**: Catalog's Promote container (`PostingOpportunitiesCard`, `services.tsx:1224`) becomes a per-listing ON-RAMP into Distribute — keep the timely nudges, move channels to the ONE hub, no second share surface. (8) **Measurement stays on Analytics/Earnings** — Distribute deep-links, never grows its own analytics (ruling 22d posture). (9) **Transport & Logistics**: the capture step ALREADY EXISTS (`ServiceForm.tsx` step 2, `showLogisticsCapture:1232`, migration 195) and all 11 D7 fields write — **but 10/11 have NO server reader** (only `changeCutoffHours` is consumed, `deposit.service.ts:94`); T1 polishes the step (Wave 1), T2 wires the consumers (Wave 2). **BUILD ORDER — two waves:** Wave 1 (mostly reuse, low risk): **C1→C2 · C3 · C4 · D1→{D2,D3,D4,C6} · T1**; Wave 2 (money/data, higher care): **T2 · B1 (travel-surcharge, a §14 money lane) · B2 (demand/gap, §13 real-data-only)**. Dependencies: C1 gates C2; D1 gates D2–D4 and C6; B1 before T2's travel-pricing reader. **GOVERNANCE (every lane):** §13 honesty, §14/§19 on new client-reachable fields, publish-trap on every column, caption-hold on Distribute; each lane = ledger note + full battery + guards + tsc ratchet + committed proofs + seam verification.
