## Guard registry (reality as of 2026-08-04 — ruling 27 note: the protocol name-checked six from memory; reality has eleven)

In-CI guards (name → script → CI location → **negative space**).

**Read the last column first (ruling 43).** Every guard here passes TRUTHFULLY within its predicate and
says nothing whatever outside it. Three holes were found in a single audit behind three green checks,
so a green result means *green-within-stated-bounds* and nothing more. A guard whose negative space is
not stated is being read as coverage it does not provide. When you EXTEND a guard's reach, revisit its
predicates in the same change (protocol advisory) **and** update this column.

| Guard | Script | CI | Negative space — what the predicate does NOT cover |
|-------|--------|-----|-----|
| mint-path owner tripwire + trackingNumber tripwire (rulings 10, 17) | `scripts/check-trip-mint-owner-access.cjs` | build.yml | Covers the KNOWN mint call sites by name; a trip minted through a new helper, a raw `INSERT INTO trips`, or a seed/migration is invisible. Proves an owner row and a tracking number are WRITTEN, never that either is CORRECT. |
| claims-only lookups | `scripts/check-claims-only-user-lookups.cjs` | build.yml | Scoped to `claims.sub` IDENTITY reads only. **A `claims.role` read is entirely outside it** — which is why `/api/provider/bookings`'s stale session-snapshot role (audit AC-2) passed unseen. It also says nothing about whether the resolved id is AUTHORIZED for what follows. |
| money-endpoints (+ commission-literal + **privileged-field** mass-assignment passes: rate, ruling 42; **payment-identity, ruling 46**) | `scripts/check-money-endpoints.cjs` | build.yml | `req.body` check is LINE-LOCAL — a body value read on one line and used on another is invisible. Scans only `server/routes` + `server/services` + `server/routes.ts`; `server/jobs`, `server/utils`, `server/storage.ts` and all client code are out of scope. `MONEY_OP_RE` is a NAME heuristic, so money moved through an indirectly-named helper is not recognised as a money handler. Handler ranges end at the NEXT route registration, so a body read in a helper declared between two routes is misattributed. The commission-literal pass knows only the values 0.9/0.1/0.75/0.25 in commission/payment/fee/payout/checkout-NAMED files. The mass-assignment pass reads `shared/schema.ts` only — a privileged field on a HAND-WRITTEN zod object, or in another schema file, is out of scope — and it knows exactly TWO classes: rate columns named rate/share/commission/split, and payment-identity columns spelled `stripe<Thing>Id`/`paymentIntentId`. **Every other privileged column a denylist schema forgets — an amount, a `status`, an authorization grant (`#PS16`) — is still reachable and still invisible here; that is the standing class of ruling 46, whose answer is pick-based schemas (`#PS18`), not a wider grep.** Its parse detection also keys on the `insert*Schema` NAME, so a DERIVED schema (`.pick()`/`.extend()` under another name, e.g. `createBookingRequestSchema`) is outside the pass — covered instead by the B6 assertion on its exact key set. **Nothing here proves the resolved rate is CORRECT — only that the client did not choose it.** |
| unmounted-routers | `scripts/check-unmounted-routers.cjs` | build.yml | Proves a router is MOUNTED, not that its routes are REACHABLE: Express is first-registered-wins, so an inline duplicate of the same path silently shadows a mounted router's copy (the `routes.ts:953` mount order is load-bearing and unguarded). Says nothing about auth, ownership or whether any endpoint has a consumer. |
| linkage-preservation | `scripts/check-linkage-preservation.cjs` | build.yml | Static check on the linkage fields it enumerates; a new linkage column, or one dropped at runtime rather than in source, is outside it. Proves the field is CARRIED, never that the value is right. |
| upsell-trust-contract | `npm run test:upsell-contract` | upsell-trust-contract.yml | Unit-level over the upsell engine's own contract; no DB, no route, no cross-surface behaviour. Does not cover the query service or any placement/pricing path that consumes its output. |
| noop-migrations | `scripts/check-noop-migrations.ts` | (Replit workflow gate) | **Not in GitHub CI** — a Replit workflow gate, so it does not run on a PR from another surface. Detects an empty/no-op migration; says nothing about whether a migration is CORRECT, idempotent, or safe against the deploy-push CHECK trap. |
| fee-config-parity | `scripts/verify-fee-config-parity.ts` | selection-controls-gate.yml | Compares the two fee-config representations for AGREEMENT; if both are wrong in the same way it passes. Does not reach `fee_bands` resolution at charge time, nor any per-service override. |
| migration-ledger idempotency | `scripts/verify-migration-ledger.ts` | selection-controls-gate.yml | Ledger/registry consistency only. Does not execute migrations, and cannot see an object the deploy-push drops because `shared/schema.ts` never declared it (the index/table trap). |
| fee-literal grep (phase2-fee-gate) | `scripts/phase2-fee-gate.sh` | build.yml (wired by ruling 27; predicate fixed + self-tested by ruling 43) | A GREP over source text: it cannot see a rate that arrives at RUNTIME — a client-supplied rate column (this is exactly how MI-1 hid), a DB row, an env var. Passes A/C match a FIXED value list, so a fee literal of an unlisted value is invisible. Pass B needs the number on the SAME LINE as a fee-ish identifier, so a rate assembled across lines, held in a map literal (e.g. `AFFILIATE_MARGIN_DEFAULTS`'s per-partner values), or named only `share`/`split`/`margin` is out of scope. `.test`/`.seed`/migration/config paths are excluded by design. Lines carrying `fee-literal-debt:#<task>` are REPORTED but do not fail. |
| chain-integrity | `server/migrations/__tests__/chain-integrity.test.ts` | build.yml (wired by ruling 27) | Verifies the registry chain's shape and ordering; does not APPLY the migrations, so a statement that fails against real data (the publish-time CHECK trap) passes here. |
| decision-guards ledger lint | `scripts/check-decision-guards.cjs` | build.yml (ruling 26) | Checks that a `[guarded: X]` tag corresponds to an actual `run:` command somewhere in CI. It cannot tell whether X's PREDICATE is any good — that gap is precisely what ruling 43 exists for. `deferred:<lane>` only warns. |
| matrix-lint | `scripts/check-coverage-matrix.cjs` | build.yml | Lints the matrix DOCUMENT (shape, expiry tags, ruling-37 wave namespacing) over `NAMESPACE_SCAN_FILES`. It never runs a single assertion, so a matrix row can be perfectly formed and completely untrue. |
| env-allowlist | `scripts/check-env-allowlist.cjs` | build.yml | Checks env-var NAMES against the allowlist. Says nothing about whether a variable is SET, valid, or pointed at the right environment — `DATABASE_URL` being absent is invisible to it. |
| **fee-resolution-authority** (rulings 47, 48, 51; **D6 attribution ruling 61**; **1C direct lane ruling 69 disposition 6**) | `server/__tests__/fee-resolution-authority.db.test.ts` + `server/__tests__/rails-attribution.db.test.ts` | `fee-resolution-authority-gate.yml` (two steps, one job) | Proves RESOLUTION and — since ruling 61 — the ATTRIBUTION half of the D6 chain, never the CHARGE. Since ruling 70 it also covers the DIRECT lane (A10–A13) — but still only its RESOLUTION and its refusal behaviour, never that `/api/checkout` writes what it resolved, and it says nothing about the D3 traveler fee, which ruling 69 disposition 6 deliberately does not start billing on the direct path. It asserts that `fee_bands` decides the rate (A1 — a band edit reaches the next resolution with no service-row touch), that rails is min(band, rails) and waives the traveler fee, that the traveler-fee cap is band data, that an entity override is stamped with a NULL `band_id`, and that a bandless category THROWS. It says **nothing** about whether a resolved rate is ever WRITTEN to `fee_ledger`, whether the ledger balances per booking (that is the invariant gate, not this one), or about any money path beyond provider commission and the traveler service fee — the other **11 of 12** census paths are entirely outside it. It also cannot see the CHARGE: both files exercise services directly, so a caller that resolves correctly and then charges something else is invisible here — including `/api/checkout` itself, whose wiring of the rails decision into the per-item split is covered by NO assertion in this gate. On the D6 half (rails-attribution.db.test.ts, ruling 61) it proves that a valid owner-matched ref selects the rails lane with the waiver, that a fabricated/expired/wrong-provider/self-referral/expert-lane ref degrades to the full rate with an enumerated reason, that the rate still moves with a `fee_bands` edit under an unchanged ref, and that the rails ledger event lands exactly once with its waiver metadata. It does NOT prove that the ledger row's amount equals the amount Stripe actually captured, that a NON-rails booking writes anything at all (no writer exists — the table is a rails-only slice today), or that any surface renders the waiver.
| **payout-parity** (payout-promise parity) | `server/__tests__/payout-parity.db.test.ts` (`npm run test:payout-parity`) | `payout-parity-gate.yml` | Proves the MINTED AMOUNT equals the STAMPED `provider_earnings` figure (P1 expert-band, P2 provider-band, P3 config drift between checkout and completion). It stamps bookings via the same resolver + insurance helper the checkout route imports, but does NOT drive the HTTP route — a route change that stamps a different figure than the shared recipe is invisible here. Says nothing about payout DELIVERY (Stripe transfers), ledger balance per booking, refunds/cancellations, or double-mint on retried confirmations. |
| **omit-schema-ratchet** (ruling 46, §19, `#PS18`) | `scripts/check-omit-schema-ratchet.cjs` | build.yml | A COUNT ratchet on `.omit()`-based `createInsertSchema(...)` call sites across the two drizzle-kit schema entry files, never an identity check — it proves the aggregate denylist total never grows, not that any specific named schema was converted to `.pick()`, and a bare (no-modifier) call folds into the same denylist bucket. It never checks whether an omitted field LIST or a `.pick()`'s field list is CORRECT — only that the shape moved. Scoped to `shared/schema.ts` + `shared/guest-invites-schema.ts` only; a hand-written zod object or an insert schema in another file is outside it. A schema DERIVED via `.pick()`/`.omit()` on an existing insert-schema VARIABLE (ruling 46's `createBookingRequestSchema` shape) does not match the `createInsertSchema(...)` call-site pattern and is not counted in either direction. It does not know whether a schema is ever `.parse()`d from a request body — that reachability judgment stays with `check-money-endpoints.cjs`'s schema-mediated pass.

Named guard candidates (not yet guards — MISSING until in CI): single-writer-per-event-type (11) · log append-only route inventory (per 12/18) · no-second-scheduling-heuristic (22 remediation) · apply-never-writes-routing_status grep · **`scripts/invariants.mjs` (exists in the repo, in NO workflow and NO package script — per ruling 27 it is MISSING, not a guard; provider-sigma AB-3 / `#PS9`)**.

## Decision #39 — Stripe-only KYC/KYB; Persona KYB retired; no Plaid for Experts

**Date:** Aug 2026

**Decision:** All provider identity and business verification flows now run exclusively through Stripe.

- **Expert identity** → Stripe Identity (unchanged).
- **Provider business verification** → Stripe Connect Express onboarding; Stripe performs KYB during the Express flow and signals the result via `account.updated` webhook. The `businessVerificationStatus` on `service_provider_forms` is derived from `account.charges_enabled`, `account.details_submitted`, and `account.requirements.disabled_reason`.
- **Persona KYB retired** — `POST /api/identity/business/create-inquiry` and `POST /api/webhooks/persona` both return 410 Gone. The `PERSONA_*` env vars are no longer required.
- **No Plaid** — the payout identity path relies solely on Stripe Connect; Plaid is not added to the platform.

**Why:** Stripe Express already performs KYB as part of its onboarding. Running a separate Persona check was a redundant compliance burden, added friction, and required maintaining a second HMAC-verified webhook surface. Centralising on Stripe reduces the external vendor surface to one, simplifies the deploy-time secret checklist, and keeps the compliance signal (KYB result) co-located with the payment rail (Stripe Connect).

**Publish gate (F2):** Service creation/update now enforces `identityVerificationStatus === 'verified' AND businessVerificationStatus === 'verified'` server-side (403 + `VERIFICATION_GATE` code) and in the ServiceForm UI (banner + disabled Publish button).

## Protocol advisories

Process lines that are not themselves rulings but bind the same way. Each cites the concrete incident that produced it.

- **Extending a scan's reach requires revisiting its predicates in the same change.** A scan's filters silently encode
  the assumptions of the *original* scope; widening what it looks at without re-reading what it filters on turns those
  assumptions into false findings. **Incident — the PR #435 legacy-drift near-miss (ruling 40, commit `056f5f44`):**
  the reconciliation job's legacy loop keys on `charge.metadata.bookingId` (**singular**, the legacy convention) and
  compares against an id set built only from the legacy `bookings` table. Cart PaymentIntents carry
  `metadata.bookingIds` (**plural**). Adding cart-rail coverage without revisiting that loop's predicate would have made
  **every cart purchase** fall through into the legacy `stripe_charge_no_booking` branch and be indicted as legacy
  drift — a detector manufacturing a platform-wide false alarm about the primary checkout. The guard is the deliberate
  `if (!bookingId) continue;` line at `server/jobs/stripeReconciliation.ts:612`, with the reasoning recorded in the
  source comment at `:605-608`. Applies to CI grep gates as much as to runtime scans: a gate whose predicate was written
  for one naming convention keeps passing truthfully while missing everything written in another
  (provider-sigma Phase 0, MI-3 and AC-2). | provider-sigma lane Phase 0, rider (c) |

| 81 | 2026-08-12 | ratified (design; decision-maker approved) — build is lane **B1**, Wave 2 | **TRAVEL-SURCHARGE = PROVIDER-CHOSEN MODE, per listing (ratified design; §14 money lane).** Rather than the platform mandating one surcharge shape, the provider picks a **`surcharge_mode`** per listing — the platform-pattern already used for `booking_mode`/`pickup_coverage_mode`/`transport_provision`. **The four modes:** (a) **`none`** (DEFAULT — every existing listing; no surcharge); (b) **`flat`** — one fee when the pickup is OUTSIDE the coverage radius (binary in/out); (c) **`zones`** — a provider-drawn base area + N ordered surcharge tiers (each a distance ring + a fee, as child rows on the `service_route_points`/`service_attestations` pattern), the pickup mapping to the tier that CONTAINS it; (d) **`per_km`** — a provider rate × the STRAIGHT-LINE distance from the pin, labeled "as-the-crow-flies, not driving." **NEVER-CLOBBER (ruling 62 posture):** switching the mode changes only what RENDERS/applies; the other modes' config is preserved, never nulled. **THE HONEST RAILS that bind EVERY mode (non-negotiable):** (1) **triggered ONLY by the traveler's CONFIRMED pickup address at booking** — a real location the traveler provides, NEVER invented; no pickup address ⇒ no surcharge; (2) **SERVER-DERIVED at checkout (§14)** from the listing's mode + config + that address — never off `req.body`; the amount is the eighth instance the §14 rule guards against, so `scripts/check-money-endpoints.cjs` must stay green; (3) **DISCLOSED as its own checkout line** ("Travel surcharge — $X"), the F1 fee-disclosure posture (ruling 80 / `FEE_SEMANTICS_DETERMINATION.md`), so the traveler sees it before paying; (4) **§13:** `flat`/`zones` decide by WHICH RING CONTAINS the address (honest containment, NO computed distance); `per_km` is straight-line, explicitly labeled, NEVER a driving time/distance; (5) **it is LISTING CONFIG, not a platform fee** (§8 — no fee_bands involvement; the provider sets the amounts/rings/rate) but it CHANGES THE CHARGE, so it is a money lane and rides the §14/§15 checkout discipline. **SCOPE:** place-anchored listings with pickup/transport on; the map surcharge ring (mockup `2caaa7c9`) stays DISPLAY-ONLY. **FLEXIBILITY BONUS:** an optional **outer bound** ("won't travel beyond X km") that doubles as BOOKING ELIGIBILITY (a pickup beyond it cannot book), reusing the coverage radius as the ceiling. **PUBLISH-TRAP:** new column(s)/child-table declared in `shared/schema.ts` + registered (migration 205), additive-nullable, app-enforced enum (no DB CHECK). NEVER a per-lat/lng heat cell — only neighborhood/market centroids. **PRIVACY posture:** server-side aggregation ONLY (counts per bucket); no individual traveler row/coords ever reaches the client, so there is no address leak by construction. **HONEST EMPTY vs WITH-SIGNAL:** empty ⇒ a "based on N real searches" honesty line + a "Not enough signal yet … nothing is estimated or interpolated" card and no map; with-signal ⇒ tinted neighborhood centroids sized by real search count + square gap markers on real centroids, a demand/gaps panel with the real counts, the unplaceable disclosure, and ODbL "© OpenStreetMap contributors" (§20, required wherever the Leaflet/OSM map renders — kept). | 86 | 2026-08-12 | [advisory] | **RULING 85 DEFECT FIXED — the office-location pre-fill no longer stamps a non-place-anchored listing (client-only; §13).** **THE DEFECT (design-audit finding, verified in code).** Ruling 85's NEW-listing seed effect in `client/src/components/ServiceForm.tsx` copies the provider's saved account `officeLocation` into `formData.locationPoint` and marks the pin **touched** so it actually SAVES — but the effect (and the submit-time send it enables) carried **NO delivery-method condition**. The Meeting Location card — the ONLY surface that renders a pin at all (the `LocationPointPicker` **and** ruling 85's "Pre-filled from your office location" note both live inside it) — renders only under `needsMeetingPoint` (`in-person`/`hybrid`). So a provider creating a **pdf / call / voice_notes / async_messaging** listing had their office coordinates written onto a listing with no location, **with no UI anywhere showing them**: a location the provider never saw and never confirmed *for that listing*, `location_precision='exact'` and all — §13's exact prohibition (a coordinate asserted on a surface that never displayed or confirmed it). **THE FIX — gate the SEND, not just the seed.** The payload now includes `locationPoint` only when `locationPointTouched && isInPerson`; `isInPerson` is the SAME predicate as `needsMeetingPoint`, so the pin is sent exactly when the card that shows it was rendered. **Clobber-safe by OMISSION, never by `null`:** `extractServiceLocation` (`server/utils/service-location.ts`) rule 3 — an absent `locationPoint` key touches NO coordinate column (create ⇒ stays NULL; edit ⇒ stored point untouched) — while rule 4 makes `null` an explicit CLEAR. Omitting is therefore the never-clobber shape and the reason the gate lives at the send rather than sending `null`; an edit-mode round trip on a listing that later switched to pdf keeps whatever it had, matching the D7 keys-omitted posture already in the same payload builder. The pin also keeps its ONE write rail (ruling 22) — nothing about `extractServiceLocation` changed. **RATIFIED BEHAVIOR UNCHANGED:** a NEW in-person/hybrid listing by a provider with a saved office location still gets the pre-filled pin, still shows the note, still saves, still fully overridable/removable. **PROOF.** `server/__tests__/provider-office-location.db.test.ts` **P5** (suite now 9/9): with the office SET, a create carrying **no** `locationPoint` key lands `latitude`/`longitude`/`location_precision` all **NULL** (no silent office stamp), while the in-person create carrying the seeded point lands the coords with server-derived `'exact'`. **STATED BOUNDS (§18d):** the defect and its fix are in the CLIENT, so this proves the SERVER half — that each of the two payload shapes produces the right row — by simulating both; it does not execute the React component and so cannot itself prove which shape the client picks per delivery method. Keep-green: attestations 25/25 · logistics-capture 15/15 · never-clobber 4/4 · protected-rail 13/13 · publish-verification-hold 9/9 · f2-verification-gate 11/11; five guards exit 0; tsc 170 = baseline; lockfile 0. **NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43):** no schema, migration, endpoint or server change; no second pin-write rail; the seed effect itself is untouched (it still seeds so the picker OPENS pre-filled the moment a provider switches a draft to in-person) — only the SEND is gated. Cites `client/src/components/ServiceForm.tsx`, `server/utils/service-location.ts`, `server/__tests__/provider-office-location.db.test.ts`, CLAUDE.md §13. |

Free text is NEVER parsed (no fuzzy match, no city-name substring, no city-centre default — `utils/service-location.ts` rule 5, one lane over), a client-sent `city` is DROPPED, and no structured source ⇒ NULL ⇒ honestly ABSENT from every market page rather than guessed into one. The market read prefers the column with the old free-text `ilike` as the GRANDFATHERING fallback (`city = X OR (city IS NULL AND location ILIKE %X%)`), so a listing whose structured city is Osaka can no longer be pulled onto Kyoto's page by its prose. The "No stay found in Kyoto" half is the same read: `productShape` now rides the payload and the Stay spine routes `property`/`property_room` (it dropped every provider service), and the 4-service filler cap is now scoped to the mixed "all" feed — a spine chip is a deliberate search, not a sample. **(B5.)** Timing/capacity/booking-rules follow the SHARED `needsScheduling` predicate (call + video join in_person/hybrid — they already needed slots by D2 and were already scored on availability); transport/pickup/surcharge stay place-anchored-only. `isPlaceAnchored` ⊂ `needsScheduling`, so an in-person listing sees exactly what it saw. Ruling 58's upload rail gets its FIRST client caller: create-mode says "Save Draft first, then upload" (no invented draft, no fake success); edit-mode POSTs raw bytes, and after an upload the save OMITS `serviceFile` entirely so an empty box can never clobber the `objstore:` key the client is deliberately never shown. Pasted-URL fallback and its `protected: false` labeling untouched. **(B10 — visibility ONLY, zero money change.)** No rate, amount or resolution order is touched (§8/§18 — the fee lanes own that): the owner health rail gains an UN-SCORED notice ("no category → the platform default commission band applies" — un-scored deliberately, because the property/bundle builders ask for no category and D3 forbids failing a provider on what the product gives them no way to fix), and the admin listing view marks the row. Deep link uses the `?param=` convention this console already has (provider inbox/settings `?tab=`): `?property=<id>` opens the editor at its basics, `?property=<parent>&room=<id>` at the **Rooms step** with that room focused. **(3) THE BACK DOOR IS CLOSED** — `/provider/services/:id/edit` reached by deep link, stale bookmark, history entry or hand-typed URL with a property/property_room id renders an honest interstitial ("a room has no delivery method or service checklist of its own") linking to the property editor; the questionnaire is **never** rendered, so its answers can never be saved onto an accommodation row. **The guard predicate is `productShape` read off the OWNER-GATED `GET /api/provider/services/:id` row — server-derived, never from the URL, a query param or any client-supplied value** (§14 identity posture applied to a routing decision); an unauthenticated read of that row 401/404s, so a crafted request cannot even learn the shape. | 89 | 2026-08-13 | [advisory] | **LANE FP-4 — PROVIDER-CONSOLE CONTENT-WIDTH CONTAINMENT (client layout only: no schema, no migration, no endpoint, no money surface; not one server file is touched).** **REPORTED BY THE DECISION-MAKER:** *"some console content stretches too much horizontally on wide screens"* — constrain it while staying mobile-friendly. **MEASURED BEFORE (bench `traveloure_fp4`, headless Chromium, 28 page/width cells):** at a **1920** viewport the console's content region was **1700 px** wide on Catalog, Workstation and Money — and on every other uncapped page (Dashboard, Distribute, Performance, Playbook, Business Profile) — so a single-line text input on a form-heavy surface ran most of the screen. **ROOT CAUSE: there was NO shared content container at all.** Every page rolled its own wrapper and they disagreed — most had no cap, Calendar capped at `max-w-6xl mx-auto`, Inbox and Customers at `max-w-4xl mx-auto`, and Settings at a bare `max-w-4xl` with **no `mx-auto`**, so the most form-heavy page in the console hugged the left edge with 896 px of content and 784 px of dead gutter at 1920. **WHAT LANDED. (1) ONE container, mounted ONCE:** `w-full max-w-6xl mx-auto` (`CONTENT_CONTAINER`, `data-testid="provider-content-container"`) wraps `children` inside **`ProviderLayout`** (`client/src/components/provider/provider-layout.tsx`) — the provider console's own layout, deliberately **NOT** the shared `BackofficeShell`, which the **expert** console also renders through and which this lane must not change (both it and `ExpertLayout` are byte-identical in this diff). **(2) WIDTH CHOICE, STATED: `max-w-6xl` = 1152 px, matching this console's OWN precedent** — the Calendar page already capped at exactly `max-w-6xl` — rather than inventing a value; it sits in the ratified 6xl/7xl landing zone and is the more conservative of the two for the form-heavy surfaces that are the complaint. **(3) CONFLICTING PER-PAGE WRAPPERS NORMALIZED, NOT STACKED:** Calendar, Inbox, Customers and Settings drop their own cap onto the shared one; no page now carries a second container. **(4) FULL-BLEED EXCEPTIONS — exactly TWO, and both are DECLARED through the layout's new `width="full"` prop rather than smuggled in by breaking out of the container: (a) the Catalog MAP view** (`/provider/services` list↔map toggle, ruling 22(b)) — its three-pane authoring canvas (selector rail · map · authoring cards) IS the shell width, and the **list** view of the same page stays contained (`width={viewMode === "map" ? "full" : "contained"}`); **(b) `/chat` via `ConsoleAwareLayout`** — a viewport-anchored two-pane messaging frame (`h-[calc(100vh-52px)]`) with its own responsive padding, rendered identically for travelers and experts, which capping for providers alone would fork into two widths. **The two `overflow-x-auto` tables were REVIEWED AND NOT GRANTED AN EXCEPTION** (Calendar's `min-w-[660px]` month grid; Business Profile's capacity table): each already manages its own scroll and scrolls INSIDE the cap, so a page-level bleed would buy nothing. **(5) PHONE BEHAVIOUR IS UNTOUCHED BY CONSTRUCTION, NOT BY LUCK.** The container is fluid up to 1152 px; the content region is the viewport minus the 220 px desktop sidebar, and the whole viewport below the sidebar's 768 px mobile breakpoint — i.e. **390 / 548 / 1060 px at the 390 / 768 / 1280 viewports, all under the cap**, which therefore first bites past ~1372 px. For the same reason horizontal PADDING was deliberately **not** made responsive: the pages keep `p-6` verbatim, because a `px-4 sm:px-6` on the container would change phone layout — the one thing this lane must not do. **NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43):** NO sidebar change; NO traveler-facing page; NO admin page; NO expert-console change; NO change to `ServiceForm` (shared with the expert console — its own `max-w-3xl` stays as-is and simply centers inside the new container, so the "768 → 1152" cell below is the CONTAINER widening, not the form); NO responsive-padding change; NO new dependency, route, endpoint, table, index, CHECK or migration, so the Replit deploy-push trap and `preflight-prod-constraints` are N/A; NO new guard (a width is a design constant, not an invariant a grep can hold). **PROOF — headless Chromium at 390 / 768 / 1280 / 1920 across Catalog list, Catalog MAP, a ServiceForm step, Workstation, Settings, Calendar and Money (28 cells before, 28 after):** (i) **no horizontal page overflow at any width in either run** — `document.scrollWidth == clientWidth` in all 56 cells, the 390 assertion included; (ii) **the 390 and 768 screenshots are BYTE-IDENTICAL before/after** (SHA-256 over all 14 mobile/tablet cells) — the mobile no-op is PROVEN, not asserted; (iii) the containment itself at 1920: Catalog list, Workstation and Money **1700 → 1152** (left offset 220 → 494, i.e. centered), Settings **896 → 1152** and centered, Calendar **1152 → 1152** (same value, now from the shared container), and the **Catalog map view stays 1700** — the exception rendering as designed. Keep-green battery serialized on the fp4 bench: **293/293, 0 fail** — fp3 8 · fp1 12 · f2 11 · publish-hold 9 · service-deliverable 9 · logistics-capture 15 · never-clobber 4 · attestations 25 · display-options 9 · content-translation 14 · short-links 12 · posting-opps 2 · sweep 9 · promotion 11 · detection 15 · provenance 7 · completion 26 · fee-resolution 13 · rails-attribution 11 · expert-booking-rate 7 · deposit-checkout 10 · market-insights 15 · booking-eligibility 11 · office-location 9 · travel-surcharge 13 · price-floor 6; plus client unit tests 64/64. All five guards exit 0 with their self-tests (check-money-endpoints, phase2-fee-gate, check-unmounted-routers, check-decision-guards, check-omit-schema-ratchet; claims-only, trip-mint and matrix-lint also green); `tsc` **170** = baseline (unchanged); `replit.local` **0**; production build clean. | decision-maker report (console over-stretch, Aug 13 2026); QA_PUNCH_LIST.md § "Fixed here (lane FP-4 — ledger row 89)"; ruling 22(b) (Catalog is the map's home); ruling 88 (FP-3, same console) |
| 90 | 2026-08-13 | [advisory] | **LANE FP-5 — THE CONSOLE-TABS P1 PACK: ONE BOOKING-VISIBILITY PREDICATE, A WIRED BELL, AN HONEST SETTINGS PAGE (no schema change, no migration, no `fee_bands` change, no new guard; the money MACHINERY is untouched — every finding here is a REPORTING defect over a correct ledger).** **EVIDENCE:** `docs/testing/CONSOLE_TABS_EXERCISE.md` (branch `lane/provider-batch-exercise`, as-of `f747d0a`) — the eight P1 findings (M1, M2, I1, X1, N1, S1, S2, I3) from the eight console tabs the batch exercise never opened. A traveler drove a real cart through the real checkout; Stripe was unreachable on that bench (`sk_test_dummy`), so `POST /api/checkout` did exactly what §15b prescribes — provisional claim written BEFORE the call, call failed, cart intact, inventory untouched (`booked_count` 0 across all five slots), row left at `status='payment_pending'` with `stripe_payment_intent_id IS NULL` and the pre-flight `stripeAttemptAt` marker stamped. **The claim machine was right every time it was checked. Then six console surfaces read that row and five disagreed about what it meant:** Today "PENDING BOOKINGS 0", Inbox "1 Pending" above "No bookings waiting", Customers "1 booking (1 pending payment)" (the only correct one), Money "Your lifetime earnings $83.60" beside a ledger reading "$0.00", Money → Earnings by Source "Direct, 1 booking, $95.00". **(1) THE ROOT FIX — `shared/booking-visibility.ts`, consumed by SERVER AND CLIENT.** Nine console modules each read `service_bookings` with their own idea of what counts; until one predicate was shared, every new tab would add a sixth answer. FOUR named sets, each with its exclusions stated as RULES not omissions: **PROVISIONAL** = `payment_pending` (an unauthorized claim — disclosed everywhere, actionable and bankable NOWHERE); **ACTIONABLE** = `pending` ONLY — `payment_pending` is excluded BY §18b/ruling 42 SD-1, since the owner rail may not move a booking out of a provisional state and a provider Accept on one destroyed reclaimable inventory with no code path to return it; **EARNING** = `confirmed` / `deposit_paid` / `in_progress` / `completed` — the only rows whose amounts may appear under an earnings/revenue label, with `deposit_paid` included at its FULL booked amounts because the deposit/balance split is the PAYMENT SCHEDULE not a re-split of the charge (ruling 72/migration 200 states earnings math reads the full amounts), and with `pending` (legacy birth state, nothing charged) and `cancelled`/`refunded`/`failed`/`disputed` deliberately absent; **RECORD** = the same membership as EARNING, named separately so a future divergence must be a deliberate edit rather than a silent re-use. **NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43):** the module says what a surface may COUNT or SUM. It is NOT a state machine, grants NO transition, and may never be used as a from-state allow-list — `checkout-claim.service.ts` remains the sole author of provisional-claim transitions and `COMPLETION_ALLOWED_FROM_STATUSES` the sole completion authority. **(2) M1/M2 — the two SERVER aggregations with no status filter at all.** `GET /api/me/earnings-by-source` grouped every row; `GET /api/me/link-analytics` summed every row per share link. The latter read $0 on the bench only by luck (that claim was untagged, `acquisition_ref` NULL) — a checkout failing the same way after a TRACKED click would report unpaid revenue against the link a provider uses to decide where to spend. Both now `inArray(status, EARNING_BOOKING_STATUSES)`. On the client, Money's `revenueBreakdown`, headline tiles, Recent Transactions and monthly series all pass through the same predicate. **The RATE was always honest** (`share/gross`, no literal — §8-clean); the AMOUNT was the lie, so §8 is untouched by this lane. The claim is not erased but DISCLOSED, in the idiom Customers invented unprompted ("not counted in any figure on this page"). **(3) X1/I1 — the three-tab disagreement, closed at the root.** Today's "0" was the CORRECT answer of the three and now reaches it through the shared predicate rather than a private filter; `GET /api/provider/analytics/dashboard` gained an ADDITIVE `summary.awaitingPaymentBookings` so the surface can disclose without banking; Customers' `pendingPaymentBookings` (the one honest counter) re-points at the shared predicate so it cannot drift from the idiom it wrote. On Inbox the tile count is now STRUCTURALLY `actionable.length` — the length of the very array the list maps over, so counter and list cannot disagree — and the claim gets its OWN tile plus its OWN read-only section carrying "Awaiting the traveler's payment — nothing for you to do", deliberately WITHOUT Accept/Decline. Inbox History moves to the shared RECORD set, which widens it by exactly `deposit_paid`/`in_progress` (real bookings that had no home) and by no provisional row. **(4) N1 — the bell is WIRED, not deleted.** The dot was an unconditional `<span>` on the SHARED `BackofficeShell`: no count, no query, no condition — lit for every provider, expert and EA, on every page, forever, including a brand-new account with nothing at all. A REAL source exists and is the console's own (`GET /api/notifications/unread-count`, the same user-scoped endpoint the traveler bell and traveler sidebar badge read), so the honest disposition was to connect it. One exported predicate `shouldShowUnreadDot`, FAILING CLOSED TWICE: zero unread ⇒ no dot, and an UNANSWERED query (loading / 401 / network error) ⇒ no dot — failing open there would restore the defect for every first paint. **(5) S1 — ELEVEN CONTROLS REMOVED, ONE WIRED; no schema change.** `provider_settings` was a closed loop (written and read only by the GET/PATCH this one page calls). Two were disproved EMPIRICALLY in the exercise, not merely by grep: `autoResponse:true` sent no auto-response to a real inquiry, and `notificationsJson.messages:true` produced no notification. **REMOVED:** Instant Booking (worse than unread — a real consumer exists but reads a DIFFERENT column, `service_provider_forms.instant_booking`, so the switch wrote the twin nobody looks at), Auto-Response, Minimum Lead Time, Target Response Time, the six-toggle Notification Preferences card (newBookings · bookingUpdates · messages · reviews · payouts · marketing), and Payout Frequency (no consumer, and it describes a scheduling mechanism that does not exist — payouts are admin-processed on request). **KEPT + WIRED:** Minimum Payout Amount (see 6). **KEPT, UNAFFECTED:** Vacation Mode (genuinely enforced server-side) and the Language card. **THE COLUMNS AND STORAGE ARE UNTOUCHED** and the §19-shaped PATCH allow-list still accepts all seven fields — this lane removes the LIE (a switch that silently does nothing), not the data; the redesign decides which features get built and a control returns WITH its consumer, never before it. Same call this page already made when it deleted its Change-Password/2FA card. **(6) S2 — one threshold, stated once: `effectivePayoutMinimumCents` = max(platform floor, the earner's own configured minimum).** The floor (`MIN_PAYOUT_CENTS`, Stripe transfer economics) is NEVER LOWERED; a STRICTER preference IS honoured; absent/garbage/negative degrades to the floor (the safe failure mode for a threshold, the posture `resolveCoordinationFee` takes on an absent band). `POST /api/payouts/request` gates on it and `GET /api/provider/earnings/summary` returns it, so the Money page prints the figure ACTUALLY IN FORCE (naming Settings when the provider's own value binds) instead of a client-side `stats.available < 10` literal. **§14 posture stated plainly:** the preference is READ SERVER-SIDE from the caller's own settings row, NEVER from a request body, and can only ever RAISE the bar on the owner's own withdrawal — it cannot lower a floor, move an amount or affect another party; `provider.routes.ts`'s header note is amended accordingly (the field now BINDS in a money decision). One consequence handled deliberately: the settings GET's no-row default moved from a cosmetic `"100"` to the platform floor, because a form pre-filled with 100 would silently impose a $100 threshold on every provider who pressed Save without touching the field. **(7) I3 — INVESTIGATED FIRST, then fixed on both sides.** There is NO separate traveler↔provider conversation system to re-point at: `/chat` IS the rail, `provider_services` is role-agnostic so a listing's owner may be a provider or an expert, and the storefront's Message CTA already proved the rail works end to end. `service-detail.tsx` linked to `/chat?provider=<userId>` — a param `chat.tsx` has NEVER read — so the most prominent way to reach a provider landed the traveler on a directory of four seeded experts with no composer. The CTA now uses the canonical `useAskExpert` rail (`?expertId=` + the documented `?name=` fallback, since `/api/experts/:id` resolves expert-family roles only and a `service_provider` target 404s there), carrying the listing name as the subject; AND `chat.tsx` accepts `?provider=` as an ALIAS for `?expertId=` so every link already in the world stops dead-ending. `GET /api/providers/:userId/public-verification` gains an additive `displayName`, derived exactly as `storefront.routes.ts` derives `earner.name` (nothing private — that name is already on the storefront and every listing card). Until it resolves the button is DISABLED rather than repeating the dead end. **PROOF.** `server/__tests__/fp5-console-agreement.db.test.ts` **9/9** (HTTP against the real dev server; negatives first: the claim contributes ZERO to earnings-by-source and ZERO to a TRACKED link's revenue — the case the bench never reached — is not "pending" on Today, is disclosed there, and Today's disclosure count EQUALS Customers'; a genuinely authorized booking appears in all of them; a crafted `POST /api/payouts/request` body naming its own minimum moves nothing). Pure units: `client/src/lib/__tests__/booking-visibility.test.ts` **11/11** (including the mutual-exclusivity invariant that makes a sixth answer unwritable), `server/__tests__/fp5-payout-threshold.test.ts` **7/7**, `client/src/lib/__tests__/backoffice-unread-dot.test.ts` **4/4**. **FIXTURE HONESTY:** the provisional row is INSERTED — driving a real checkout to the §15b state needs an unreachable Stripe, the exact bench limit the exercise recorded and declined to work around; the seeded row mirrors the captured one column for column, marker included. **NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43):** NO schema change, NO migration (so the Replit deploy-push trap and `preflight-prod-constraints` are N/A), NO `fee_bands` or rate change, NO change to the claim machine / promotion path / sweep / drift job, NO expert-console page touched (the two SHARED server aggregations and the SHARED bell are fixed for all three consoles by construction), NO new guard (a status predicate is a shared module, not an invariant a grep can hold — the module's own unit suite is the enforcement). **KEEP-GREEN BATTERY serialized on a fresh `traveloure_fp5` bench (32 suites): 336 pass, 0 unexpected fail** — fp5-agreement 9 · fp3 8 · fp1 12 · f2 11 · publish-hold 9 · service-deliverable 9 · display-options 9 · logistics-capture 15 · never-clobber 4 · attestations 25 · content-translation 14 · travel-surcharge 13 · price-floor 6 · deliverable-rail 13 · short-links 12 · posting-opps 2 · sweep 9 · promotion 11 · detection 15 · provenance 7 · completion 26 · fee-resolution 13 · rails-attribution 11 · expert-booking-rate 7 · deposit-checkout 10 · market-insights 15 · booking-eligibility 11 · office-location 9 · payout-parity 3 · payout-parity-route 7 · money-hardening 4 · fp5-payout-threshold 7; plus client units 79/79. **The ONLY failures are the two ALREADY-DOCUMENTED bench limitations**, both re-verified as unrelated: `provider-money-hardening` P1/P2 (the fresh-bench `active_provider_commission_policy='tiered'` naming no `fee_bands` row — punch-list bench observation; ruling 42's P3–P6 SD-1/AC-1 proofs are green) and the six `city-feed-card-recommendation.test.tsx` failures FP-4 verified identical on a clean tree. Five guards + their self-tests exit 0 (check-money-endpoints, phase2-fee-gate, check-unmounted-routers, check-decision-guards, check-omit-schema-ratchet); `tsc` **170** = baseline (unchanged, none in touched files); `replit.local` **0**; production build clean. | `docs/testing/CONSOLE_TABS_EXERCISE.md` (P1 findings M1/M2/I1/X1/N1/S1/S2/I3, as-of `f747d0a`); `docs/planning/QA_PUNCH_LIST.md` § "Fixed here (lane FP-5 — ledger row 90)"; §14 (server-derived money), §15b/§18b (provisional claims and the owner rail), §13 (never a fake indicator), ruling 89 (FP-4, same console) |
| 91 | 2026-08-13 | [advisory] | **LANE FP-2 — THE SERVICE-CREATION FORM FIX PACK (Package A / execution-map Wave 1: CLIENT ONLY — not one server file is in the diff, so no schema change, no migration, no endpoint, no `fee_bands` change, no money surface, and the Replit deploy-push trap and `preflight-prod-constraints` are N/A).** **SOURCES:** the Service Creation Audit (Aug 12, 2026) Package A, sequenced as Wave 1 of `docs/briefs/SERVICE_CREATION_EXECUTION_MAP.md`, with the redesign mock's "Fix pack" swatches (A1–A6) as the ratified design per item, and `docs/testing/PROVIDER_BATCH_EXERCISE.md` as the practice evidence. **SCOPE DISCIPLINE, honoured:** form fixes only — NO step reordering, NO method-first restructure, NO map-authoring move; those are Wave 2 lanes S1/S3. FP-1's, FP-3's and FP-5's closures were not redone. **(1) A1 — THE BUTTON TOLD THE TRUTH ABOUT WHAT IT DOES.** Every create is clamped server-side to a non-approved born state (F2 / migration 111 — `provider_services.approval_status` DEFAULTs `'submitted'`), yet the provider's final button read **"Publish Service"** (MEASURED on the bench before the fix) and the only statement of what would really happen was the success screen AFTER the click, while the expert branch was told up front. Now **"Submit for review"**, with the upfront notice rendered on EVERY step for BOTH roles. **THE SLA NUMBER IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT:** the mock's A1 copy says "usually within 2 business days", and Gate **G5 #7** (*"review SLA — is '2 business days' real?"*) is OPEN with the disposition *"measure first, then commit or drop the number"* — so stating one would be precisely the §13 claim that gate exists to prevent; the expert card's own unmeasured **"within 48 hours"** is removed for the same reason rather than reworded. COPY ONLY: no gate, no lifecycle change, no write-path change. **(2) A2 — THE DEAD SWITCH IS GONE, AND SO IS THE STATE BEHIND IT.** The step-4 Published/Draft `Switch` bound `formData.active`, which was never put on the create or update payload and which no gate consulted — flipping it changed nothing while reading as the control that puts a listing live. Replaced by a **read-only status pill over the real record** (mock A2: *"Draft → In review → Live … no control that pretends to set it"*), and `active` is DELETED from `FormData` so nothing can bind to it again. **(3) PACKAGE A ITEM 3 — FOUR UNREAD LOGISTICS FIELDS REMOVED, EVERY COLUMN UNTOUCHED,** each re-verified by grep at this SHA rather than trusted from the audit: **`durationMinutes`** (zero consumers on `provider_services` — every other `durationMinutes` hit in the repo is `itinerary_items`, a different row), **`bufferMinutes`** (zero), **`canAnchor`** (zero, and the label is planner jargon a seller cannot interpret), **`pickupRadiusKm`** (zero, and it was the THIRD radius input on one form — the ring travelers see, the Catalog map ring and the flat-surcharge containment test all read **`service_radius`**; what replaces it is a pointer at that number, so a provider choosing "Radius" is not left with an input that decides nothing). **ONE KEPT, WITH ITS REASON STATED: `transportProvision`** — the audit's *"one of which nevertheless decides what UI the provider sees"*. It has no data consumer, but it gates BOTH the pickup-coverage block AND the travel-surcharge block, and the surcharge is charged for real at checkout (ruling 81); removing it would have made live money config unauthorable. A field with no consumer is a defect; a field whose consumer is a live gate is not. **NEVER-CLOBBER:** all four stay in form state, loaded from the row and sent back unchanged, so an edit saves the stored value rather than nulling it — this removes the QUESTION, never the DATA, the same call FP-5 made on Settings, and a control returns WITH its consumer, never before it. The card's **disclaimer was wrong in both directions** ("these details aren't shown to travelers yet" over a card whose start window, timezone and party-size pair are what `booking-eligibility.service.ts` refuses a booking against) and is rewritten to what the card actually is. **(4) PACKAGE A ITEM 4 — THE ASTERISK SET NOW EQUALS THE ENFORCED SET, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** The predicate moved OUT of the 4,000-line component into `client/src/lib/service-form-required.ts` (pure, unit-tested) because a two-way rule stated only as prose inside that file is a rule nothing can check. **NEWLY BOUND:** **Price** (mirrors the server's `PRICE_REQUIRED` gate; PROVIDER-ONLY on exactly FP-1/B7's reasoning — only a provider's final action sends `status:'active'`, an expert's submit writes `status:'draft'` which that gate exempts; a `package_tiers` listing is judged on its LOWEST POSITIVE TIER, the same scalar the server recomputes); **required CATEGORY fields** (`category_field_schema.required` has always drawn their asterisk and nothing ever checked them); and **the attestation confirmations** (already ENFORCED by a server 403 and a disabled button, but wearing no asterisk and naming themselves only in a `title` tooltip). **NEWLY UN-ASTERISKED (the other direction):** **Description** and **Duration** carried an asterisk while NO layer required them — both columns are nullable and no publish gate reads either — so binding them client-side would have invented a block the server does not have; they now read "(recommended)", which is what they are (both are scored by the owner health rail). Draft saves stay check-free from every step, exactly as before. **(5) A3 — CATALOG MUTATION ERRORS IN HUMAN WORDS (client mapping only; NO server response changed).** MEASURED before: flipping a listing to Active without a meeting point showed the provider, verbatim, `400: {"message":"In-person services need a meeting point before publishing. Save as draft to finish later.","code":"MEETING_POINT_REQUIRED"}` — the server's sentence was already honest, merely buried inside `apiRequest`'s `"<status>: <body>"` string. New `client/src/lib/catalog-error-copy.ts` unwraps it, names the action that failed, and flags the refusals a provider fixes IN the listing editor so the toast carries the way out (the mock's "Add one →", shipped as a **Fix it** action). A `VERIFICATION_REQUIRED` refusal is deliberately NOT flagged editor-fixable — it is fixed on Provider Status — and an UNRECOGNISED code is never guessed at (§13): it simply gets no affordance, and the server's message still reaches the provider. **(6) A4 — DELETE ASKS FIRST.** MEASURED before: one click took the catalog from 13 listings to 12, with no dialog and no undo, while the Workstation's property and bundle deletes two clicks away both confirm. Catalog now uses the same `AlertDialog` and NAMES the listing. **SCOPE STATED:** the plain confirm ONLY — the "refuse + archive when travelers have already booked it" half is **gap #18**, a Wave 3 lane with a server side, so the dialog claims nothing about bookings it has not checked. **(7) ITEM 7 — THE BUNDLE EDIT LINK CARRIES ITS ID.** `listingEditHref` resolved a bundle to a bare `/provider/workstation` — the right page with the identity dropped on the floor, landing the provider on a list of every bundle they own to find the one they had just clicked (FP-3's own note filed this as the FP-2 item). New `bundleEditorHref` → `/provider/workstation?bundle=<id>`, consumed on the same `?param=` convention the property editor already uses, and waiting for BOTH the bundle and service reads before opening — the builder prefills its component list from `eligibleComponents`, so opening earlier would silently drop every component. A bundle id this account does not own resolves to nothing and SAYS so in the bundles section (§13), never opens another one. **(8) ITEM 8 — THE DUPLICATE QUESTIONS, MERGED; THE MAPPING PER PAIR.** **DURATION** — survivor is the free-text question on Details, which writes **`deliveryTimeframe`**, the string the traveler detail page, Discover, the storefront and Catalog cards, the admin queue and `envelopeFromProviderService` (which parses minutes out of that very text) all read; the structured "Duration (minutes)" duplicate is removed because its column has no reader. **CAPACITY** — survivor is the **party-size pair**, the numbers the SERVER enforces (`booking-eligibility.service.ts` refuses a booking outside `party_size_min`/`party_size_max`); removed is **"Max Concurrent Clients"** on step 4, a second capacity number three steps and one vocabulary away whose only consumer is the Catalog card's "Up to N" chip — rendered beside a Users icon, so it reads as a group size too. Column untouched; the chip still renders for rows that carry a number; concurrency returns as its own question with the Wave-2 Capacity step. **TRANSPORT — A RECORDED DEVIATION FROM THE MOCK.** Mock fix **A6** proposes ONE question in the `transportProvision` vocabulary and drops the yes/no disclosure. **Ruling 62** (the `transportProvisionEnum` block in `shared/schema.ts`) states the two columns answer DIFFERENT questions and must NOT be collapsed — `transport_provision` is *"how does the traveler get to the start"*, `transport_provided` (migration 119, which carries a real DB CHECK) is *"once you've met, do you drive them"* — and `transport_provided` is the one of the pair that is actually READ (`service-detail.tsx` renders it, `envelopeFromProviderService` carries it), while `transport_provision` has no consumer beyond this form's gating. Deriving either from the other IS the merge that ruling forbids, and §13 forbids inventing the half that is not entailed. So BOTH are kept and BOTH are now asked in ONE block ("Getting there"), in one order, with the distinction said out loud — the yes/no question MOVED out of the Meeting Location card, which is what made them read as one question answered twice. **The mock's A6 swatch should be amended to match** (mock-parity rule); it is a scratchpad artifact, not a committed file, so this row is the record. FILED: a traveler renderer for `transport_provision` belongs to **T-REP (#13)**, the ratified "render or stop collecting" lane, not here. **PROOF.** Pure units: `client/src/lib/__tests__/service-form-required.test.ts` **11/11** (negatives first — a complete listing is missing nothing; no un-enforced field can enter the list, asserted structurally by the predicate not receiving one; an EXPERT does not inherit the provider-only publish gates; a remote listing is never asked for a meeting point or a deliverable; a required BOOLEAN category field is never "missing" because `false` is an answer, not silence), `client/src/lib/__tests__/catalog-error-copy.test.ts` **8/8** (negatives first — the server's sentence survives word for word with no envelope leaking, a NON-JSON body is never turned into an invented reason, an unrecognised code gets no "Fix it", a verification refusal is not editor-fixable), `client/src/lib/__tests__/property-editor-link.test.ts` **8/8** (extended with B1/B2 for the bundle link, including that a crafted id is URL-encoded). **HEADLESS before/after at 1280 over the same fixtures on a fresh `traveloure_fp2` bench, every fact flipping in the intended direction:** review notice 0→1 · final button "Publish Service"→"Submit for review" · dead switch 1→0 with the status pill 0→1 · `Description *` and `Duration *` true→false · duration-minutes / buffer / can-anchor inputs 1/1/1→0/0/0 · pickup-radius input 1→0 with the coverage-source note 0→1 · max-concurrent input 1→0 · Catalog toast raw JSON blob → human title + the server's own sentence + "Fix it" · Delete 13→12 listings with no dialog → dialog shown and **16→16** (nothing deleted until confirmed) · bundle deep link no dialog → "Edit bundle" prefilled with the right bundle · and a structural probe showing BOTH transport controls inside `logistics-section-transport` with **zero** transport controls outside it. **KEEP-GREEN BATTERY** serialized on the fp2 bench (32 server suites + 12 client suites): ****436 pass, 2 fail** — server suites 336/338 and client units **100/100** (fp5-agreement 9 · fp3 8 · fp1 12 · f2 11 · publish-hold 9 · service-deliverable 9 · display-options 9 · logistics-capture 15 · never-clobber 4 · attestations 25 · content-translation 14 · travel-surcharge 13 · price-floor 6 · deliverable-rail 13 · short-links 12 · posting-opps 2 · sweep 9 · promotion 11 · detection 15 · provenance 7 · completion 26 · fee-resolution 13 · rails-attribution 11 · expert-booking-rate 7 · History gained Cancelled/Refunded filter buttons alongside the existing Confirmed/Completed ones. | 96 | 2026-08-13 | [advisory] | **LANE QA-2 — NOTIFICATION DURABILITY + A SLOT-LEAK INVESTIGATION (one migration, no CHECK; the money/claim machinery is untouched — §15/§15b/§15c/§18b are precedent, not surface here).** **FINDING A (durability) — INVESTIGATED then fixed at the smallest shape.** The owner rail's accept/decline (`PATCH /api/provider|expert/bookings/:id/status` → `storage.updateServiceBookingStatus`, §18b's atomic-conditional writer) committed the status flip and wrote the traveler's notification as two separate statements — a crash in the gap left a status change with no notification, and the atomic transition guard turned a client retry into a bare 409 that could never repair it. The actual write IS a plain in-app `notifications` row insert (no email/push in this path today), so per the ruling-80 "flip-and-mint in one transaction" precedent (generalized), the fix is NOT an outbox worker — the notification insert now lives INSIDE the SAME transaction as the status flip in the canonical writer, plus an idempotent dedupe: nullable `notifications.dedupe_key` (migration 209), a PARTIAL UNIQUE index (`WHERE dedupe_key IS NOT NULL`, the migration-155/203 precedent — legacy NULL rows never collide), `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, key shaped `booking:<id>:<event>` (e.g. `booking:<id>:accepted`, `booking:<id>:cancelled`). Same treatment for BOTH the accept path (which previously wrote NO traveler notification at all — a bigger gap than the filed one) and the unpaid-decline path (which had one, just not durably). If a future external send (email/push) is added here, §15b's posture applies unchanged: it stays best-effort AFTER commit, never inside the transaction — the durable guarantee this lane adds is the in-app row only. **FINDING B (the missing proof) — CLOSED, negatives first.** `server/__tests__/qa2-notification-slot-durability.db.test.ts` **5/5**: P1 pending→confirmed produces EXACTLY ONE notification; N1 a CONCURRENT second accept (the §18b atomic-conditional race LOSER — 5 parallel callers via `Promise.allSettled`) produces ZERO additional notifications and leaks no error (0 rejections, exactly 1 winner); N2 a crash-simulating RETRY (re-running the exact same transition with NO restrictive `expectedFromStatuses` — the real shape both `POST /api/bookings/:id/cancel`'s non-refund branch and a lost-response client retry take) is a no-op: one notification, one slot release, no throw. **FINDING C (slot leak) — INVESTIGATED BEFORE BUILDING; mostly already covered, one real gap fixed.** Traced `voidClaim` (the §15b TTL sweep, `checkout-claim.service.ts`) — it ALREADY releases the claimed slot atomically with the void. Traced `refundServiceBooking` (`stripe-payment.service.ts:1012-1019`) — it ALREADY releases the slot after a successful Stripe refund. So the stale comment in `payments.routes.ts` ("release on abandoned/refunded bookings is a filed follow-up") is CLOSED with a pointer to both — not re-filed, not re-built. The GAP the investigation found: the NO-REFUND cancel/decline branches (the owner rail's unpaid decline, and the traveler's own non-refundable `POST /api/bookings/:id/cancel`) call `storage.updateServiceBookingStatus` directly and never touched the slot — a paid, slot-bound booking cancelled with no refund due (a non-refundable policy, or a decline whose Stripe lookup could not confirm payment) permanently stranded its claimed capacity, with no code path to return it (the exact §18b SD-1 shape, one layer over). **FIXED IN the canonical writer, never a second reclaim rail (§18c):** on the booking's FIRST transition into `cancelled`/`refunded`, if the row carries a `slotId`, the same floor-at-0 / re-open-if-under-capacity release `voidClaim`/`refundServiceBooking` already use runs atomically in the SAME transaction as the status flip. Proven DB-level (same suite): P2 a paid, slot-bound booking cancelled with no refund gives its slot back; N3 a capacity-3 slot releases exactly one seat (the other booking's seat stays held) and a retried refund (unconditional guard) does not release a second seat. **MIGRATION.** `server/migrations/209_notification_dedupe_key.sql` — additive nullable `notifications.dedupe_key` + the partial UNIQUE index, NO CHECK constraint. Column and index DECLARED in `shared/schema.ts` (publish-trap rule — an undeclared index is what the deploy-push drops, the `sb_idempotency_key_idx` lesson). Proven idempotent in a ROLLED-BACK transaction (run twice in one `BEGIN…ROLLBACK`; both statements hit `IF NOT EXISTS`/"already exists, skipping"). **NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43) — what this lane did NOT do:** no second reclaim rail beside the claim machine (§18c) — the slot release is one more atomic step inside the EXISTING canonical writer, never a new scheduler/worker; no external-send-inside-a-transaction (this path has no email/push today; the posture is noted for if one is ever added); no outbox worker (not needed — the durable guarantee is the in-app row, written same-transaction, deduped by a DB constraint); the paid-refund cancel branches (owner-rail refund, traveler-refund) are UNTOUCHED — they already release the slot via `refundServiceBooking` and already notify AFTER that external call completes, which is the correct best-effort-after-commit shape and not this lane's target; no change to the claim machine / promotion path / sweep / drift job. **KEEP-GREEN**, serialized on a fresh `traveloure_qa2` bench (port 5010, `OBJECT_STORAGE_DRIVER=memory RATE_LIMIT_LOOPBACK_SKIP=1 SESSION_SECRET=bench` stub Stripe keys, `scripts/seed-ci-test-users.ts` run first): qa2-notification-slot-durability **5/5** · sweep **9/9** · promotion **11/11** · detection **15/15** · provenance **7/7** · completion **26/26** · booking-eligibility **11/11** · fp5-console-agreement **9/9**. Five guards exit 0 (check-money-endpoints, phase2-fee-gate, check-unmounted-routers, check-decision-guards, check-omit-schema-ratchet); `tsc --noEmit` **170** = baseline (unchanged, zero new errors in any touched file); lockfile `replit.local` **0**; production build (client + server) clean. | `docs/planning/QA_PUNCH_LIST.md` § "Fixed here (lane QA-2 — ledger row 96)"; ruling 80 (the flip-and-mint-in-one-transaction precedent this lane generalizes to notifications); CLAUDE.md §15/§15b/§15c/§18b/§18c; migration-155/203 (the partial-unique-index precedent); `server/services/checkout-claim.service.ts` (`voidClaim`), `server/services/stripe-payment.service.ts` (`refundServiceBooking`) |

| 121 | 2026-08-15 | [advisory] (test-strategy P0 executed: "commit the rulings 112–119 regression specs"; the audit it triggered is the substance of this row) | **SPEC ROT IS A STANDING CLASS — a committed spec that no workflow runs is not coverage.** Executing the strategy's #1 P0 surfaced that only **~14 of ~53** Playwright specs are referenced by any workflow, and that FOUR provider-console specs were **dead on arrival** for two independent reasons the green CI could never show: (a) all four authenticate as `kyoto-interpreter@traveloure.test`, which its owning vendor seed creates with **no password** and no other seeder touches — every run 401'd at the door, in every environment, since the day they were written; (b) two of them assert a seeded fact that never existed (`Business Document Translation` as approved+draft, while the vendor seed inserts every service `approved`+`active` in one shared insert). PRs #484–#487 then renamed the testids and copy those specs assert on, and all four merged 54/54 green. **Rulings that follow:** (1) a spec is only coverage once a workflow runs it — new console specs land WITH their gate (`provider-console-gate.yml`, which carries the spec-file-existence guard so a rename fails loudly instead of silently reducing the gate to zero assertions); (2) a fixture a spec must log in as needs its credentials in `e2e-test-accounts.seed.ts` — the idempotent FIXTURE_LOGIN_BACKFILL fills a NULL password/handle only, never overwrites and never steals a claimed handle; (3) **a spec needing a not-live listing CREATES a throwaway (born `submitted`, migration 111) and DELETEs it — it must never pause a seeded row**, because an un-verified provider cannot PATCH `status` back to `active` (403 VERIFICATION_GATE), making the pause a one-way door that corrodes the shared fixture every run; (4) a repaired assertion asserts the NEW ruling, so a repair is coverage gained, not an assertion downgraded. Also landed: step-4 authoring coverage (D-10/12/13/16) with autosave proven by round-trip (reorder → re-read → order persisted WITH coordinates intact), and en⇄ja key parity (286 keys, 0 gaps, mutation-proved red-able). Filed: `catalog-preview-toggle` (same false premise, ungated pending the same fix) and a sweep of the ~34 remaining ungated specs. | this session; test-strategy artifact 6d2bbfba; rulings 112–119; PRs #484–#487 |

| 2026-08-16-ledger-ids | 2026-08-16 | [guarded: check-decision-guards] | **LEDGER IDS ARE DATE-SLUG KEYS; THE NUMERIC SERIES 1–122 IS FROZEN (docs/tooling only — no product code, no schema, no migration, no endpoint).** **THE PROBLEM, measured not guessed:** ruling 35's "the appender claims the next free number at write time" makes a collision STRUCTURAL — two lanes opened on the same day both take the next integer, and whichever merges second renumbers AND chases every cross-reference that cited the number. In one night, three lanes (`#489` spec-rot, `#490` catalog-map M3, `#491` coverage sweep) collided on rows 120/121/122 while touching **no common product code**; every merge conflict of that night was `docs/DECISIONS.md` and `docs/planning/QA_PUNCH_LIST.md` and nothing else. **THE FIX:** new rows are keyed `YYYY-MM-DD-<kebab-slug>` (this row is the first). Two lanes cannot collide unless they pick the same slug on the same day — and if they do, the guard now FAILS rather than leaving a human to notice. **THE NUMERIC SERIES IS FROZEN, NOT DEPRECATED:** ids 1–122 are cited across the briefs, CLAUDE.md and the guard registry; they are never renumbered and never reused, and old rulings keep being cited by number. **GUARD (`scripts/check-decision-guards.cjs`, already CI-wired as `decision-guards-lint`):** its row predicate accepts all three id shapes (date-slug, numeric, the closed `R-[A-Z]` Console Realign letters), the slug alternative first so `\d+` cannot half-match a year; and the duplicate check — which previously covered **numeric ids only** — now covers **every** shape, which is the half that makes the scheme enforceable rather than merely conventional. Per §18d a predicate change ships with committed `--self-test` fixtures: three new ones (a slug row's `[guarded]` tag lints like any other, a DUPLICATED slug fails, a malformed slug row is rejected not skipped), all **mutation-proved** — removing slug support from the predicate, or narrowing the duplicate check back to numeric-only, each turns the self-test red. **NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43):** this does not touch the QA_PUNCH_LIST's own append-at-EOF collisions — those resolve trivially as "keep both sections" and cost judgment only when a row NUMBER is involved, which is what this removes; it does not renumber, move or edit a single existing row; and it changes no product behaviour of any kind. | ruling 26 (append-only ledger), ruling 35 (the superseded numbering rule), ruling 43 (negative space), §18d (fixtures ship with predicate changes); the three-way collision on PRs #489/#490/#491, Aug 15–16 2026 |

Building `itinerary_item_events`/`expert_assignment_events` beside it is exactly the parallel-scorer class R6/L6 forbid. **THE RULING (verbatim):** "R15 — R10 amended: extend `item_transition_log`; no new event tables. Add `item_removed` to the event-type vocabulary; write it same-transaction at every hard-delete path through the existing `logItemTransition(tx, …)` module (append-only contract untouched). Market derives at the rollup layer via `trip_id → trips.market_slug` — never denormalized onto diary rows. R3 assignment events extend the same vocabulary if needed beyond `assignment_accepted`. `expert_assignment_events` and `itinerary_item_events` are not built. 2B.3's snapshot-only restriction is lifted: funnel/time-in-stage metrics may compute from existing diary history. Gates transfer: rollback test proves delete + `item_removed` row are all-or-nothing; grep confirms every hard-delete path logs." **WHAT MOVES:** R10's "(b) sibling tables" disposition is SUPERSEDED — the envelope-compat intent (a future unification is mechanical) is satisfied a fortiori by there being ONE table. R2's `itinerary_item_events` and R3's `expert_assignment_events` are NOT built. The genuine gap R2 named — the removal signal — is closed by adding the `item_removed` event type to the existing vocabulary, not a new table. **R11 capture-first UNCHANGED:** the removal clock only starts once `item_removed` is written, so 2A.1 is still the first capture step. **L6 UNCHANGED:** market is derived at the rollup via `trip_id → trips.market_slug`, never denormalized onto a diary row (a diary row is history, not a report). **2B.3 SNAPSHOT-ONLY RESTRICTION LIFTED:** funnel and time-in-stage metrics MAY compute from the existing diary history in 2B (the trail is same-transaction and append-only, so it is a faithful record, not a reconstructed guess — §13). **GATES TRANSFER (not new gates):** (1) a rollback test proves the hard-delete and its `item_removed` row are all-or-nothing (same-transaction contract); (2) a grep/prior-art gate confirms EVERY hard-delete path logs `item_removed` — the same coverage discipline R2 required of the four writers, now applied to the delete paths. **HUMAN READ before merge:** the `item_removed` vocab addition + every same-transaction delete-path write (the R10 "both event-table migrations" human-read item collapses to this, since no migration is now needed — the column exists). **PRIOR-ART LESSON (folded forward per the decision-maker):** future Phase 0 briefs carry a mandatory prior-art grep over the mechanism domain (`transition|log|event|audit`) before ANY new event/audit table is proposed — three times this lane the keyword sweep missed a live mechanism (`demand_signal_events`, the dead-table graveyard, `item_transition_log`); grepping the domain up front is cheaper than escalating mid-build. | 2A.1 discovery (`server/services/item-transition-log.service.ts`, `itemTransitionLog` migration 171, `routing.routes.ts` transaction writers); | 2026-08-17-delete-archive | 2026-08-17 | [advisory] (executes the Gate G5 #18 ratification — "refuse + archive, mirroring the shipped withdraw precedent". **RATIFIED AS BUILT, decision-maker, Aug 17 2026** ("ratify the code to enable product deletion"): deletion is ENABLED behind the booking guard as coded — sold/delivered history keeps refusing with Archive offered, the withdraw-precedent side of the flagged mock-bullet divergence; the mock's "once delivered, deleting becomes possible" bullet stands amended and holds only for bookings that end cancelled-unpaid) | **GAP #18 BUILT — DELETE-WITH-BOOKINGS REFUSES AND OFFERS ARCHIVE (server guard + ONE archive write path + client refusal dialog; NO schema change, NO migration, NO CHECK — `provider_services.status` is a plain varchar and `'archived'` is a new app-enforced value riding the EXISTING `status='active'` public-read filters, so the publish-trap and preflight are N/A).** **THE HOLE:** `service_bookings.service_id` is ON DELETE CASCADE and `DELETE /api/provider/services/:id` checked nothing — deleting a booked listing silently destroyed the booking rows a traveler's receipt, review and the earner's payout all point at. **(1) ONE assessment, both delete rails:** `assessServiceDeletion` (`server/services/service-delete-guard.service.ts`) is called by the provider DELETE and the expert `service-listings` DELETE before any destroy. Vocabulary lives in `shared/booking-visibility.ts` beside the console's other predicates (never a hand-kept copy): `OPEN_BOOKING_STATUSES` (`payment_pending`, `pending`, `confirmed`, `deposit_paid`, `in_progress`, `disputed` — the listing still owes the traveler an answer, a delivery or a resolution; a §15b in-flight claim blocks because promote/void needs the row to exist) ⇒ 409 `HAS_OPEN_BOOKINGS`; `TRANSACTED_BOOKING_STATUSES` (`completed`, `refunded`) ⇒ 409 `HAS_BOOKING_HISTORY` — the withdraw precedent's "sold history is never deleted" (c2), which the ratified G5 line itself cites. `cancelled` (unpaid — a charged cancel lands in `refunded`) and `failed` block NOTHING: a listing whose rows never transacted stays genuinely deletable. **FLAGGED DIVERGENCE (ratify or amend):** the mock's archive modal bullet "Once the last booking is delivered, deleting it becomes possible" is NOT honored for delivered bookings — delivery makes a row `completed`, i.e. transacted history the CASCADE would erase, so the precedent wins over the bullet; the bullet holds only for bookings that end cancelled-unpaid, and the client renders no false promise. **(2) ARCHIVE, the honest alternative:** `POST /api/provider/services/:id/archive` is the ONE write path into `status='archived'` (meeting-pin one-write-path posture): every public read already filters `status='active'`, so the listing leaves search/storefront/marketplace immediately with NO new predicate anywhere; existing bookings stand; a property's rooms archive with it (separate bookable rows must not stay live under an archived parent). Archived is TERMINAL except deletion: the PATCH rail refuses `status:'archived'` as a value (`ARCHIVE_VIA_ENDPOINT`) and refuses every edit on an archived row (`LISTING_ARCHIVED`) — "nobody can book it again" is a promise, not a pause. **(3) CHECKOUT ARM:** `/api/checkout` refuses any cart line whose service is `archived` BEFORE any slot claim / booking row / Stripe call (the bundle/room re-verify posture) — narrowly scoped to `archived`; paused/unapproved mid-cart behavior is deliberately unchanged. **(4) CLIENT (Catalog):** the delete flow opens the mock's "This listing cannot be deleted" dialog on either 409 — body sentence and counts are the SERVER'S OWN message (§18 rule 1 — never restated client-side), ✓-bullets in the mock's words, "Archive listing" action; an "Archived" filter chip renders only when archived rows exist; archived rows render no dead controls (no storefront switch, edit, promote, availability, card settings — Delete, still guarded, is the one action). **NEGATIVE SPACE (ruling 43):** no standalone Archive menu action (the mock offers archive only behind the refusal); no un-archive (not ratified); the expert rail's "Cannot delete approved services" 400 stays; `bookSlot`/claim machine untouched; gap #16 (photos) remains the last open G5 item. **PROOF:** `service-delete-archive.db.test.ts` D1–D7 (7/7: open blocks, claim blocks, history blocks, dead rows don't, mixed counts honest, archive leaves the public read); tsc = baseline (zero new); production build clean; money-endpoints + unmounted-router + decision-guards + undeclared-tables green; catalog-error-copy 8/8. | Gate G5 #18 (SERVICE_CREATION_EXECUTION_MAP, ratified Aug 13); ready-made withdraw precedent (c2); §13/§15b/§18 rule 1; ledger 2026-08-16-console-conformance-fixes (chip provenance); QA_PUNCH_LIST §G5 batch |

Ruled and landed: the fallback now selects `price, provider_service_id`; a **linked** item re-derives `finalPrice` from `provider_services.price` (the catalog row — the §14 server record; the link is only ever set for services actually offered to the AI, validated at insert); an **unlinked** item (pure AI invention) is **REFUSED** with an honest error — apply-to-cart already skips unlinked items, so nothing legitimate books through this path, and refusal is the §13-honest failure mode. A vanished/unpriced catalog row also refuses (a charge can't rest on a missing record). **Visibility, not silence:** when the stored LLM figure differs from the catalog price, a `console.warn` provenance line records both — so how often this path would have mischarged is observable. Scope boundary: the `itinerary_items.estimated_cost` branch above it is UNCHANGED — that is the canonical traveler-owned trip record and the established §14 posture; widening this ruling to it would change ordinary trip-item booking behavior and was not ratified. The §19c guard's stated blind spot (amounts are invisible to the schema-mediated pass) is unchanged — this fix is the manual catch that blind spot predicts. | resolves `2026-08-22-optimizer-catalog-honesty` (c); §14 (provenance, not just transport); §13 refusal posture; `server/services/booking.service.ts` |

The autoApply(cart) path's states and redirects are untouched (scope boundary, same as the review-first row).
